# Spec: explicit-plugin-and-attach-config

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| Status | verification |
| Scope | config |
| Priority | P0 |
| Release reason | Security and configuration correctness of shipped behavior: closes the authorization bypass every plugin identity holds on `dispatch-command` (`aaa.ReservedInternalPrefix`), replaces the ExaBGP-derived `plugin { }` and per-peer `attach process` grammar with one message bus on which every participant registers what it sends and receives and the operator overrides it with `to`, `from` and allow-lists, shows every registered default beside what the operator wrote, converts all repository configs, tests and docs with no hybrid layering, and fixes aliased plugin state-key and doctor snapshot keying |
| RFC gaps | - |
| Depends | - |
| Phase | 8/8 (Closure) |
| Handoff | - |
| Design tier | strongest tier |
| Implementation tier | development tier |
| Updated | 2026-11-25 |

Bucket: `plan/release/fixes/`. One spec carries the whole work (Thomas, transcribed, 2026-11-23: "I think we should have one spec because all the work is very interdependent and while it's harder to do, it can ensure that everything works before it's committed."). The two former child specs, `spec-bus-2-implied-display-and-grant-refusals.md` and `spec-bus-3-external-command-gating.md`, are absorbed here whole (goals, acceptance criteria, censuses, tests and the owned journal row) and are removed by the main session through the commit tool. The work is cut into phases inside this spec ("Implementation Steps"), never into child specs.

Recovery after compaction: `.claude/rules/post-compaction.md`.

## Task

Replace `plugin { internal ...; external ...; hub ...; }` and BGP's `attach process <name> { ... }` (including inline `run`/`use` and the dead `content` container) with one message bus. Everything that takes part in the bus is a participant: a BGP peer, a BGP group, a plugin, a program Ze did not ship, an ExaBGP script. A participant registers a name based on its configuration path and says which messages it sends and which it receives. Events and commands are one thing: messages, each with a name, content and tags, the sender being a mandatory tag. With nothing written by the operator, what the participants registered is what runs. The operator overrides it near the emitter, in a `bus` block on the participant's own configuration: `to` says where this participant's messages go, `from` says who may address it, and a send and a receive allow-list say which message names it may send and receive. The bus compiles what is written on the participant and what is written in the `bus` section into one filter per participant and enforces it when it receives a message. One display shows the registered defaults beside what the operator wrote. The same filter is applied to every engine operation a plugin calls, which closes the authorization bypass plugin identities hold today. The work also fixes the aliased-plugin identity defects on the state and doctor surfaces, and converts every config, test and page in the repository.

### Owner decisions governing this spec

Thomas speaks through voice transcription. Short quotes are his transcribed words; each row gives the reading taken. The M and E rows were given on 2026-11-23 after the path-flow design of this spec, and they replace it. The S rows are earlier rulings still in force.

| # | Decision, as read | His words (transcribed) where short |
|---|-------------------|--------------------------------------|
| M-1 | Two things exist: what is said and who it is said to. A message has a name, content and tags. Tags filter and limit spread. A destination optionally limits a message to one part of the bus instead of broadcasting | "We have two things: what we say and who we say to." |
| M-2 | Everything that registers on the bus gives its name, based on its configuration path, and says what it is willing to receive and send. The sender is a mandatory tag. Events and commands are one thing | "Everything registering to the bus for message, like a peer, should give its name, which will be based on the path" |
| M-3 | A path is written in the short form. Every configured participant uses its exact YANG path (`/bgp/peer/<name>`, `/bgp/group/<group>`, `/bgp/group/<group>/peer/<name>`, `/bgp/route-server`, `/bus/endpoint/<name>`). Matching any BGP peer (static or dynamic) by remote IP uses `/bgp/ip/<address>`, registered by BGP as a secondary lookup backed by `connection/remote/ip` and `connection/remote/range`, so a peer named like an IP or named `ip` never collides and configured paths stay aligned with YANG (refined 2026-11-23, Option A) | "Accept the short form."; "allow both"; then "option A" |
| M-4 | Default wiring comes from the code: plugins register under predictable names what they send and receive. Publications are registered before receptions, and a reception with no sender yet is valid and dormant. With nothing written, the registration is accepted as is | "By default we should otherwise accept what the plugin sends us as requested events" |
| M-5 | Configuration is an override written near the emitter: a `bus` block on the participant's own configuration. One YANG structure, defined once by the bus, is attached by augmentation to every node registered as a participant, and is inherited from group to peer. A participant's block goes where its configuration already is: the route server's under `bgp { route-server { } }` (`ze-rs-conf.yang` augments `/bgp:bgp`, verified by the main session); a plugin with no configuration of its own, such as the route reflector, uses its bus endpoint | "The to should be on the route server and route reflector plugins yang config" |
| M-6 | `to` says where this participant's messages go. A written list replaces the registered default entirely. There is no add form. Present and empty means nobody | "Yes, it replaces." |
| M-7 | `from` says who may address this participant. Absent means everybody. A member's list replaces its group's. Present and empty means nobody | "If not set, the from should be everybody. But it must exist to allow you to limit who you allow." |
| M-8 | Both `to` and `from` exist as a block-level list covering every message and as a per-message list that wins for that message | His "Option A" |
| M-9 | A send allow-list and a receive allow-list of message names also exist: absent means everything declared, empty means nothing, and there is no deny form. They work with `to` and `from`. The bus is the filter: enforcement is at the bus when it receives the message from a participant | "The bus should be a filter of what is allowed to be sent on the bus. Instead of limiting the message being generated, we can be limiting the message being received by the bus" |
| M-10 | There are two places to write: on the participant, and centrally in the `bus` section (his example: a third-party plugin Ze did not write, limited from the bus side). Both are compiled into one filter per participant. A disagreement between the two places is refused at commit, naming both lines. A destination written for a message the same participant's allow-list excludes is the same refusal (proposed by the main session; he did not object) | "The configuration should generate the filter as one structure and make sure there is no conflict between what is set up on the bus, the bus side, and the plugin side" |
| M-11 | The bus does not arbitrate between participants. No exclusive-role rule, no commit refusal and no silent winner in the bus. Two plugins answering the same message is acceptable. That a route server and a reflector do not both replay the same routes to one peer is the business of those plugins | "By default, both plugins should have an imply rule of to /bgp. And by default, the BGP should send to both because they will register on the broadcast."; "So I see no issue with the design." |
| M-12 | Converting today's `attach process` one to one is wrong: attach adds from silence, and `to` is exclusive. The converter writes nothing by default. Only configs and tests that prove a difference are restated, through the test judge | - |
| M-13 | Enforcement of the allow-lists covers every engine operation from the start, and the authorization-profile bypass by plugin identity is closed in this spec | - |
| E-1 | ExaBGP compatibility is a normal plugin, run as a normal program: a piece of code that transforms the ExaBGP script format to the native one. The `exabgp { bridge { } }` config section and the compiled-in internal bridge mode are removed. Each ExaBGP script is an ordinary bus endpoint whose `run` line is the existing external wrapper, and its scoping is the common `bus` block on that endpoint. This supersedes the earlier "the bridge keeps its `feed` blocks" and, where ExaBGP scoped a process per neighbor, "the migration writes no list" | "I thought ExaBGP will be a normal plugin, run as a normal application, and will not need a separate section on the configuration."; "Yes, that will remove the ExaBGP bridge. That's why I decided to have a bus design." |
| M-14 | Group to peer inheritance is per item. A line a member writes replaces that line of its group, an empty one replacing it with nothing; a line the member does not write still comes from its group (answers Q-8) | "If you write bus to nothing, then you replaced it with nothing." |
| M-15 | The central place, the `bus` section, reaches any participant by its path, not only a plugin's endpoint ("Option B", answers Q-3). A peer or a group may be limited in its own section or centrally; both compile into one filter; a disagreement is refused naming both lines; identical content in both places is accepted (answers Q-4) | "Option B" |
| E-2 | A participant that announces routes is treated as BGP treats a peer: the engine holds each announced route under the participant that originated it (the mandatory sender tag), and when that participant goes away its routes are withdrawn, by default, for every plugin. Two participants announcing the same route hold two entries, as two peers do, so one going away leaves the other's route in place. An option keeps a participant's routes when its program disappears; it exists for ExaBGP compatibility, `ze exabgp migrate` writes it where ExaBGP's behaviour requires, and it is not meant for general use (answers Q-E1) | "We should work the same way BGP works for the peer and consider them peers. If the session goes down, the route announced by that script should go down. As this is not the default behavior for exabgp we may want an option to keep the route in the routing table on software disappearance. It should only really be used for ExaBGP." |
| E-3 | The standalone form of the ExaBGP translator (run by hand on stdin and stdout, with no connection to Ze) stays, as a help for users testing a script. Two runners remain: the endpoint form and the standalone form (answers Q-E6) | "Yes, keep the shell options here to help users use the program." |
| E-4 | A participant that announces routes has a virtual session, as a peer has a real one. The capabilities of that virtual session (address families, ADD-PATH, route refresh) are not configuration: the plugin states them in the plugin protocol during its startup negotiation. The bridge root's `family`, `route-refresh` and `add-path` leaves are deleted, and their content moves into what the plugin declares (answers Q-E5) | "I believe however it shouldn't be in the configuration but sent by the script. [...] So what will have been effectively part of the bridge configuration will be part of the plugin protocol we use." |
| E-5 | The ExaBGP API version a script writes, or is held to, is the translator's internal matter, per script. It is not shared between translators, is not a bus message and is not an endpoint setting: an `api version` line from script A changes A alone (answers Q-E2) | "An ExaBGP version API should be handled internally by the plugin code and never reach the endpoint or the bus." |
| E-6 | The ExaBGP translator mirrors exactly how the script it runs behaves: when the script exits, the translator exits, with the script's outcome. The translator no longer restarts its script; Ze's process manager restarts the endpoint, translator and script together, by the endpoint's own configured restart setting (`external { respawn }`). "The participant went away" is the endpoint's process exiting, and E-2 applies with no special case: its routes are withdrawn unless the keep option is set. The standalone runner mirrors its script the same way and writes no withdrawals to stdout when its script exits, because `OwnerLedger` is deleted whole (confirmed 2026-11-24, Option A). A restarted endpoint declares its virtual session again (E-4) (answers Q-E8) | "Make sure the translator exits also when the program exits. It should mirror exactly how the program it runs behaves. We should restart it ourselves if the configuration we have is that we have."; "Option A" |
| E-7 | The capabilities a script's virtual session declares (E-4) come from the ExaBGP configuration. `ze exabgp migrate` writes them as options on the translator's `run` line, the `--family`, `--route-refresh` and `--add-path` options the wrapper takes today (`cmdPlugin`, `internal/plugins/exabgp/main.go`), and the translator declares what they state in the plugin protocol at start (answers Q-E10, option (a)) | "The information is in the ExaBGP configuration. When the converter creates the ze file, it can add it to the command line." |
| M-17 | Every line of a participant's `bus` block is grouped by direction: `receive { allow; from; message <name> { from; tag; } }` and `send { allow; to; message <name> { to; tag; } }`. A tag filter takes its direction from the part it is written in. It replaces the flat shape everywhere; every earlier decision holds unchanged | "Good", then "Okay, look good to me" |
| M-16 | The operator can write a tag filter other than the sender: under a named message on a participant's `bus` block, a line filtering on a tag, so the participant receives that message only when the tag has one of the listed values, whatever it registered for (answers Q-7, option (b)) | Shown to him as `bus { message route-change { tag source [ bgp ]; } }`; he chose option 2 |
| M-18 | A line may name one peer admitted by a dynamic group's range, by its address (`receive { from [ /bgp/ip/198.51.100.7 ]; }`), although that peer has no configuration entry. The line is dormant until a peer with that address connects, then applies to it (answers Q-6, option (b)). Confirmed 2026-11-23 (A-19, Option A): an unconfigured `/bgp/ip/<address>` is accepted only when it falls inside a range at least one configured dynamic group accepts, and is refused at commit otherwise | "I think we should allow it."; "Option A makes sense." |
| V-1 | The message-name vocabulary (Q6 Option A, carried from the absorbed `spec-bus-3`): short tokens toward a subsystem (`system-rib:route-install`, `bgp:update`), command path prefixes toward `cli` (`"cli:show bgp peer"`) | - |
| P-1 | A pipe operation works on the answer of the command before it; it may narrow, extend, transform or deliver it, and never changes what the command does. Recorded on `docs/architecture/api/commands.md` and `docs/architecture/cli/command-verbs.md` by commit 2e8d9c853b | "It never changes what the command does" (the page's wording) |
| S-1 | The operator's bus configuration is the trust boundary. The API is the same for a compiled-in plugin and a program Ze did not ship, and no rule applies to one kind alone without a transport reason | - |
| S-2 | A compiled-in plugin runs in the daemon by default. Running it as a separate process is an operator option, and it keeps the same identity, state grants and defaults | - |
| S-3 | Offline validation starts an external program through its declaration (query mode), never its run loop. This reverses the earlier ruling that with no daemon Ze starts no external program; he confirmed the reversal is intended | - |
| S-4 | `ze start --plugin <program>` keeps working | - |
| S-5 | A route server serves every route server client connected to it (RFC 7947 Section 1); a reflector follows RFC 4456 | - |
| S-6 | ExaBGP: a script is passed only the neighbors that named it, `ze exabgp migrate` emits a full loadable config, and a script holds the whole ExaBGP command set. How it is expressed is E-1 | - |
| S-7 | Nothing implied is hidden: one display shows the registered defaults beside what the operator wrote. It shows the defaults a written list replaces, and which line produced each effective entry | - |
| S-8 | A feature's required relationship that an override removes is refused at commit with the line that completes it. AIGP being on by default implies nothing | - |
| S-9 | `ai/META-DECISIONS.md`, "CLI, config, and daemon surfaces": "What a plugin registers is not such a choice: a configured plugin receives and sends what it registered, shown as implied, and the operator writes an allow-list only to narrow it." | - |
| S-10 | `plugin { }` becomes `bus { }` and `hub` moves under it; the listener service `plugin-hub` becomes `bus-hub`. Repository configs are converted directly | - |
| Owned journal rows | The alias-keying row of 2026-11-14 and its five sibling defects, and the plugin identity that bypasses authorization profiles (2026-11-23) | "Owned journal rows" |

### The message model

| Term | Meaning | Who declares it |
|------|---------|-----------------|
| Participant | One party on the bus. It has one name, sends messages and receives messages | The owner of the configuration node: BGP for its peers and groups, the bus component for an endpoint, a plugin for its own config section |
| Name | The exact YANG configuration path of the participant, in the short form: `/bgp/peer/edge1`, `/bgp/group/transit`, `/bgp/group/transit/peer/t1`, `/bgp/route-server`, `/bus/endpoint/collector`. Any BGP peer (static or dynamic) may also be addressed by its remote IP as `/bgp/ip/192.0.2.1`, which names the same participant as its YANG path (M-3) | Derived from the schema node the owner registered and the entry's key; `/bgp/ip/<address>` is registered by BGP as a secondary lookup on `connection/remote/ip` and `connection/remote/range` |
| Part of the bus | A shorter path. `/bgp/group/transit` covers the group and every peer under it; `/bgp` covers every BGP participant | Nothing to declare: a prefix of names |
| Message | A name in a namespace (`bgp:state`, `bgp:update-received`, `bgp:update`, `system-rib:route-install`, `"cli:show bgp peer"`), content, and tags. An event and a command are both messages | The participant kind that sends it; a command path is registered by the command component under `cli` |
| Tag | A property of one message that filters it and limits its spread. `sender` is mandatory and is set by the bus from the authenticated registration, never from content. The direction of a BGP event stays a tag in the index key, as today (`deliveryKey`) | The sender's kind declares which tags a message carries |
| Destination | Optional. Absent, the message is broadcast to every participant registered to receive it. Present, it reaches one participant or one part of the bus | The sender, per message; a command's destination is the participant that owns the command |

What a participant kind registers, through one API for a compiled-in plugin (`registry.Registration`, from `init()`) and a program Ze did not ship (the Stage 1 `declare-registration` message):

| Declaration | What it states |
|-------------|----------------|
| Participant node | The schema node whose configured entries are participants of this kind (a list or a container of the registrant's own module). A plugin that registers none is the participant `/bus/endpoint/<its endpoint name>`, and a plugin loaded with no endpoint written is `/bus/endpoint/<registry name>` |
| Sends | The message names an instance may send, with the tags each carries, and for an event its default destinations (broadcast unless stated) |
| Receives | The message names an instance is willing to receive, and from which part of the bus by default (everybody unless stated) |

| Rule | Meaning |
|------|---------|
| Sends before receptions | Compiled-in sends exist at `init()`. A declared external program's sends are read when `ze` starts, before the full parse. So the set of messages on the bus is complete before any reception is resolved, and the catalog reaches every plugin in its Stage 2 configure message, beside `Claims` |
| A dormant reception is valid | A registered reception whose sender is absent (a build without BGP, a plugin configured later) resolves to nothing, is not an error, and is shown as dormant |
| A process speaks only as itself | The bus stamps `sender` from the registration the connection authenticated as. A process names as sender only a participant it registered, as BGP does for its peers |
| Run-time participants | An in-daemon owner adds participants while it runs on the publication it already makes (BGP's `AddPeer`, `createDynamicPeer`, `completeRemovals`). A peer added later is covered by the registered wiring with no config change |
| No arbitration | The bus delivers to every participant the filters admit. It holds no exclusive-role rule and picks no winner (M-11) |

When a message passes from sender S to receiver D. All seven are resolved when the index is built or the config is applied, never per message, except condition 7 for a tag whose value differs per message, which costs one comparison of interned ids for a receiver that filters on it ("Tag filters"):

| # | Condition | Default with nothing written |
|---|-----------|------------------------------|
| 1 | S's kind declared sending the message | The registration |
| 2 | S's send allow-list admits the name | Everything declared |
| 3 | S's `to` for that message covers D | The registered destinations: every registered receiver for a broadcast, the owner for a command |
| 4 | D's kind declared receiving the message | The registration |
| 5 | D's receive allow-list admits the name | Everything declared |
| 6 | D's `from` for that message covers S | Everybody |
| 7 | Each tag filter D holds for that message admits the message's value of that tag | No tag filter: every value |

### The `bus` block on a participant

One YANG grouping, defined once by the bus module, is attached by augmentation as a container named `bus` to every schema node registered as a participant node. The set of nodes derives from the registrations: compiled-in ones before the schema is built, an external program's with its YANG at Stage 1 (joined at `ze start` before the full parse, and taken in query mode with no daemon). BGP's own YANG module names nothing of the bus, and no engine or config code names a feature.

Every line is grouped by direction (M-17, approved by Thomas on 2026-11-23, replacing the earlier flat shape of `to`, `from`, `send { allow }`, `receive { allow }` and `message <name> { to | from }`):

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|-------|------|---------|
| `receive > allow` | leaf-list of message names or `none` | Which messages the participant may receive. Absent: everything its kind declared. Present: the listed names only. `none`: nothing (combining `none` with other entries is refused at commit; Q-1). No deny form (M-9) |
| `receive > from` | leaf-list of paths (short form) or `none` | Who may send to this participant, for every message. Absent: everybody. Present: those senders only. `none`: nobody (M-7, Q-1) |
| `receive > message <name>` | list keyed by message name, holding `from` and `tag` | For that message only: a `from` that wins over the block-level `from` (M-8), and inbound tag filters |
| `receive > message <name> > tag <tag-name>` | list keyed by tag name, each a leaf-list of values (`receive { message route-change { tag source [ bgp ]; } }`) | The participant receives that message only when the message's value of that tag is one of the listed values, whatever it registered for. Absent: every value. Several `tag` entries must all admit it (M-16) |
| `send > allow` | leaf-list of message names or `none` | Which messages the participant may send, same three states: absent (all declared), listed names, or `none` (M-9, Q-1) |
| `send > to` | leaf-list of paths (short form) or `none` | Where its messages go. Absent: the registered destinations. Present: the complete destination set, replacing the registered one; nothing is added to it (M-6). `none`: nobody (Q-1) |
| `send > message <name>` | list keyed by message name, holding `to` and `tag` | For that message only: a `to` that wins over the block-level `to` (M-8), and outbound tag filters |
| `send > message <name> > tag <tag-name>` | as above | Outbound: only messages whose tag value is listed are put on the bus. A tag filter takes its direction from the part it is written in |

Worked shapes Thomas was shown and accepted: one participant receiving different messages from different parts (`receive { message update-received { from [ /bgp/group/customers ]; } message state { from [ /bgp/group/transit ]; } }`); a plugin sending different messages to different places (`send { message update { to [ /bgp/group/customers ]; } message refresh { to [ /bgp/peer/edge1 ]; } }`); different groups each with their own block. Recorded limitation, not a question: one message block holds one `from` and one tag filter applied together, so the same message with different tag filters depending on the sender is not expressible; it is left out until a need appears.

| Rule | Statement |
|------|-----------|
| Inheritance | Per item (M-14). A group's block applies to each peer under it. A line the member writes (`send > to`, `receive > from`, a `send > message <name>` or `receive > message <name>` entry, `send > allow`, `receive > allow`) replaces the group's line of the same field and the same message whole, and is never merged with it; a present and empty line replaces it with nothing. A line the member does not write still comes from its group. The grouping needs a schema-level "replaces whole" marker on each of these lines, because `deepMergeAt` merges containers key by key and the only replace-whole mechanism today is the literal BGP map `replacePaths` |
| Precedence | For one message: the member's per-message list, then the member's block-level list, then the group's per-message list, then the group's block-level list, then the registered default |
| A path covers | The participant it names and every participant under it. A peer written by name and by address is one participant |
| Allow-list and lists together | A message must pass the allow-list and the `to`/`from` lists of its direction. A `send > message <name> > to` written for a name the same participant's `send > allow` excludes, or a `receive > message <name> > from` for a name its `receive > allow` excludes, is refused at commit (M-10) |
| A message addressed to participants | A message whose destinations are participants (an event, `bgp:update`, `bgp:raw`, a route a script announces toward peers) reaches each destination its sender's `to` covers. A `to` naming a peer covers a message addressed to that peer; the model's condition 3 and S9 already state it |
| A command addressed to its owner | A `cli` message's destination is the participant that owns the command, so `to` cannot widen it, and a `to` that does not cover the owner means that command is not sent; the display says so. Where a `cli` command dispatched via `dispatch-command` / `dispatch-command-args` also selects peers (such as ExaBGP's `send bgp <sel> update text ...`, `request peer <sel> flush` or `clear bgp rib <sel>`), `send > to` judges the peers the command selects (`filterPermittedPeers`) and the `cli:` prefix is checked by `send > allow` only, never requiring `/bgp` in `to` (Q-T5, option (a)). `update-route` is judged as `bgp:update` alone (and `bgp:refresh` when `PeerMarker` is `borr` or `eorr`), never as a `cli:` path (Q-T4, option (b)) |

### Two places to write, one filter

| Place | Written as | Use |
|-------|------------|-----|
| On the participant | `bus { ... }` inside its own configuration: a BGP group or peer, the route server's `bgp { route-server { } }` section | The override sits beside what it concerns |
| On the bus, for an endpoint | The same fields directly inside `bus { endpoint <name> { ... } }` | The place for a plugin with no configuration of its own (the route reflector, a collector), and the bus side of any plugin: his example, a third-party program Ze did not write, kept from sending anything or limited in what it receives |
| On the bus, for any participant (M-15) | The same fields inside `bus { participant <path> { ... } }`, the path in the short form of `to` and `from` (`bus { participant /bgp/group/transit { receive { from [ /bus/endpoint/collector ]; } } }`) | A peer or a group limited centrally rather than in its own section. The path names one participant or a part of the bus; a path naming no participant is refusal 1. The keyword `participant` is the form Q-3 option (b) proposed and Thomas chose; an endpoint's own `send`/`receive` fields stay beside `participant /bus/endpoint/<name>` as two spellings of the endpoint's bus side, judged by refusal 6 (Q-10, option (a)) |

| Rule | Statement |
|------|-----------|
| One filter | For each participant the bus compiles the registration, the bus-side fields and the participant-side block into one filter. Delivery, the send check, the display and the doctor reconcile all read that one structure |
| Conflict | Where one participant is written in two places (its own section and a `participant` entry; a plugin's config section and its endpoint entry), the same field for the same message written in both with different content is refused at commit. The message names both lines and says to keep one. Identical content in both is accepted and shown once, with both lines as its origin (M-15) |
| A `participant` path covering several participants | A `participant /bgp/group/transit` entry applies to the group as a group's own block would, and so to each peer under it by inheritance (M-14); the member's own line then replaces it item by item. A conflict (refusal 6) is judged at each participant between lines of the same rank (its own section against a `participant` entry naming it; Q-10, option (i)) |
| One verdict | Every refusal below is the same at `ze config validate`, editor commit, reload and `ze start` |

Refusals at commit. Each names the line, the offending value and what is valid:

| # | Refused | The message lists |
|---|---------|-------------------|
| 1 | A `send > to` or `receive > from` entry (block-level or per message) that names no participant and no part of the bus. A `/bgp/ip/<address>` inside a range some dynamic group accepts names a peer that may connect, and is accepted as a dormant line (M-18); an address outside every such range is refused (M-18) | The participant nodes that are registered and the configured entries of the list the path reaches; for `/bgp/ip/<address>`, the configured peer IPs and the ranges the dynamic groups accept |
| 2 | A `send > to` entry under which nobody registered to receive that message | Who registered to receive it |
| 3 | A `receive > from` entry under which nobody declared sending that message | Who declared sending it |
| 4 | A `send > message` name or `send > allow` entry the participant's kind did not declare sending, or a `receive > message` name or `receive > allow` entry it did not declare receiving | What the kind declared in that direction |
| 5 | A per-message `to` or `from` written for a message the same direction's allow-list excludes | Both lines |
| 6 | The same field written in both places with different content | Both lines |
| 7 | An override that removes a relationship a configured feature requires | The feature, the participant and the completing line ("Feature requirements") |
| 8 | A commit that deletes an entry a `to` or `from` names | The line that names it, so the operator changes it first |
| 9 | A `tag` line naming a tag the message's sender kinds do not declare for it | The tags declared for that message |
| 10 | A `tag` value outside the closed set the tag declares | The values of that set |
| 11 | A `tag` line that shares no value with the tag filter the participant's own registration states for that message | Both: the registered filter, and the line, so the operator writes a value the registration admits or removes the line |

### Tag filters

Owner decisions on Q-7 and on the block's shape (M-16, M-17, 2026-11-23): the operator can write a tag filter on the participant's `bus` block, under a named message, in the part that gives its direction: `bus { receive { message bgp:route-change { tag source [ bgp ]; } } }` receives that message only when the tag has one of the listed values, whatever it registered for; `bus { send { message bgp:update { tag <t> [ ... ]; } } }` puts on the bus only the messages whose tag value is listed.

| Point | Design |
|-------|--------|
| What exists | A subscription already filters on tags under other names: `Subscription.Direction`, `PeerFilter` and `PluginFilter` (`plugin/server/subscribe.go`), with direction a closed set in the index key (`deliveryKey{ns, et, dir}`). A publisher declares the tags its messages carry (the "Tag" row of "The message model"); a plugin's own registration may already narrow a reception by a tag, as a direction or a peer selector does today |
| Grammar | `receive { message <name> { tag <tag-name> [ <value> ... ]; } }` (inbound) and `send { message <name> { tag <tag-name> [ <value> ... ]; } }` (outbound), a list keyed by tag name with a leaf-list of values. Condition 7 of "The message model" is the inbound filter; an outbound filter is judged with condition 2, before the message reaches the bus. Values are typed by the tag's declaration: a closed set (direction, a source protocol) or an open one (a peer selector) |
| Inheritance | Per item, like `to` and `from` (M-14): a member's `tag <t>` line for one message replaces its group's line for the same tag and message whole; a tag it does not write comes from its group |
| Commit validation | Refusals 9, 10 and 11 above, with one verdict at validate, editor commit, reload and start. A `tag` line under a message the participant does not receive is refusal 4 |
| The central place | The same `message <name> { tag ...; }` inside `bus { endpoint <name> { } }` and `bus { participant <path> { } }`; a disagreement with the participant's own section is refusal 6, identical content is accepted (M-15) |
| Compilation | Condition 7 of "The message model". The operator's line narrows the registered filter: the effective set is the intersection of the registration's values and the line's. For a tag whose value is a closed set fixed per sender or per message kind, the intersection is resolved into the receiver slices when the index is built. For a tag whose value differs per message, each message carries the value as an interned id, and the receiver's set is compared only for an entry that filters on that tag; a receiver with no filter on it pays nothing |
| No shared value | Refused at commit (refusal 11) where the registration states its filter as a pure value. Where a plugin narrows a reception only at run time, through its ready RPC, the commit cannot see it: the reception is then shown dormant by the display and by `show doctor`, naming the two filters |
| Display | `\| implied` prints the effective tag set beside the line that produced it and the registered values it narrowed; `show event delivery` shows the tag condition on each edge it applies to |
| Performance | `TestBusDeliveryAllocatesNothing` gains a tag-filtered receiver: zero allocations per operation, and no comparison for a receiver without a tag filter |

### The endpoint, the hub and process identity

`bus { endpoint <name> { ... } }` states one program on the bus. It replaces `plugin { internal }` and `plugin { external }`.

| Field | Type | Description |
|-------|------|-------------|
| `name` | string (key), `ze:validate "external-plugin-name"` | Endpoint name; the participant is `/bus/endpoint/<name>` |
| `internal` | string (`ze:validate "internal-plugin-name"`) | Registry name of a compiled-in plugin. Its identity is the compiled registration, whatever the endpoint is called. Mutually exclusive with `external` |
| `process` | enumeration `in-daemon` (default) or `separate`, valid with `internal` only | `separate` makes Ze fork its own binary for that plugin over the TLS connect-back. The operator writes no command line. Refused for a plugin whose registration sets `RunsInProcessOnly` |
| `external` | container (`run`, `respawn`, `timeout`, `on-exit`) | A program Ze did not ship. Its identity is the endpoint name, and it takes nothing from a compiled registration of the same name. `on-exit` (`withdraw` default or `keep`) keeps its originated routes when it exits, for ExaBGP compatibility (E-2, Q-E11) |
| `encoding` | enumeration `json` or `text` (default `json`) | Wire encoding of events delivered to this endpoint (replaces `encoder`) |
| `send`, `receive` | The bus grouping (direction-grouped, M-17) | The bus-side filter of this plugin |

`bus { hub { server ...; client ...; } }` holds the TLS listener and managed-config client blocks moved from `plugin { hub }`.

### Enforcement at the bus: every engine operation

The filter is applied where the bus receives a message from a participant. For events that is the delivery index. For everything a plugin calls on the engine it is the shared dispatch path every operation already passes through, so the socket rail and the DirectBridge rail cannot differ.

| Rule | Design |
|------|--------|
| A field on the entry | Each engine operation states the message it carries, or `none` with a written reason. This covers the entries of `engineOps` (`internal/component/plugin/server/dispatch_registry.go`) and the operations their owners register through `RegisterEngineOp` (`register_engine_op.go`), which has no such field today |
| `none` is a statement | `cancel-request` cancels the caller's own request. The `state-*` operations are governed by state key grants. `report-issue` and `metrics-publish` act on the caller's own records. Each reason is on the entry |
| An undeclared entry is refused | A test walks both registries and fails on an entry with neither a message nor a reason. A new operation cannot ship ungated by omission |
| Commands by path | `dispatch-command` and `dispatch-command-args` carry the message `cli:<command path>`. The path, not the wire method, is the key. `cli` is the namespace the command component registers, with the prefix matcher `authz` uses for profiles (`Entry.matches`, `authz/authz.go`: an entry's match string is a prefix of the command at a word boundary, or a compiled regex); it is no special case in the bus. The vocabulary is V-1: short tokens toward a subsystem, command path prefixes toward `cli` |
| One allow-list for every namespace | A participant has one send allow-list, and it speaks for every namespace it declared sends in: a list holding only `bgp:update` admits that message and refuses every `cli:` path the participant declared (S36) |
| Receiving is not sending | `subscribe-events` and `unsubscribe-events` are bounded by the caller's registered receptions and its receive filter |
| Emitting | `emit-event` is refused for a message the caller did not register as a send |

Every operation and the check it makes today on its caller, read at the handler on 2026-11-23 by the agent that took the census. The last column is this design's.

| Operation | Handler (file) | Check on the caller today | Message it carries, or `none` and why |
|-----------|----------------|---------------------------|----------------------------------------|
| `state-get`, `state-put`, `state-remove`, `state-list`, `state-increment` | `opState*` (`plugin/server/dispatch_state.go`) | State key grants (`statestore.PluginKeyReadable`, `PluginKeyWritable`, `PluginList`), keyed on `proc.Name()`, the operator's alias | `none`: governed by state key grants, keyed on the registration identity after this work |
| `metrics-publish` | `opMetricsPublish` (`dispatch_metrics.go`) | None; acts on the metric set owned by `proc.Name()` | `none`: the caller's own records |
| `report-issue` | `opReportIssue` (`dispatch_report.go`) | None; reports under `proc.Name()`, rate-limited per owner | `none`: the caller's own records |
| `update-route` | `opUpdateRoute` (`dispatch_registry.go`), `handleUpdateRouteSelResultDirect` (`dispatch.go`) | `RequirePeerDeclarations`, then the command runs under `internalPluginIdentity(proc.Name())`, which `Store.authorize` allows by prefix; the BGP rails then apply the per-peer send grant (`filterPermittedPeers`). `PeerMarker` (`borr`/`eorr`) builds `request peer <sel> borr|eorr` | `bgp:update` (and `bgp:refresh` when `PeerMarker` is `borr` or `eorr`), judged without a second `cli:` check so `send { allow [ bgp:update ]; }` works; `opUpdateRoute` restricts `input.Command` to UPDATE subcommands so it cannot invoke `raw` (Q-T4, option (b)) |
| `dispatch-command` | `opDispatchCommand`, `dispatchCommandPeerResponse` (`dispatch.go`) | The prefix bypass: `internalPluginIdentity(proc.Name())`, allowed before any profile | `cli:<path>` |
| `dispatch-command-args` | `opDispatchCommandArgs`, `dispatchCommandArgsResponse` (`dispatch.go`) | `RequirePeerDeclarations`, then the prefix bypass | `cli:<path>` |
| `subscribe-events`, `unsubscribe-events` | `opSubscribeEvents`, `opUnsubscribeEvents` (`dispatch_registry.go`) | None at the operation; delivery stays bounded by the peer's grant (`PeerScopedProcs`) | The named message, judged as a reception |
| `emit-event` | `opEmitEvent` (`dispatch_registry.go`) | None; no check that the emitter owns the namespace | The emitted message, judged as a send |
| `forward-cached` | `opForwardCached` (`dispatch_cached.go`) | `RequirePeerDeclarations`, then the per-peer send grant on the forward rails | `bgp:update` |
| `release-cached` | `opReleaseCached` (`dispatch_cached.go`) | None; releases cache entries under `proc.Name()` | `none`: the caller's own cache holds |
| `relay-stored-route` | `opRelayStoredRoute` (`dispatch_cached.go`) | `RequirePeerDeclarations`, then the per-peer send grant on the relay rail | `bgp:update` |
| `select-routes` | `opSelectRoutes` (`dispatch_selected.go`) | The caller's `SelectedOwner`, the per-peer send grant, and for cancellation the destination's `update` grant (`selectedCancellationScope`) | `bgp:update` |
| `cancel-request` | `opCancelRequest` (`request_cancel.go`) | Cancels the caller's own request | `none`: the caller's own request |
| `select-refresh` | `opSelectRefresh` (`dispatch_selected_refresh.go`) | The caller's `SelectedOwner`, and `DeliveryGraph.MaySend(destination, proc.Name(), "update")` | `bgp:update` |
| `route-install`, `route-remove` | `opRouteInstall`, `opRouteRemove` (`dispatch_route.go`) | None | `system-rib:route-install` |
| `route-metrics` | `opRouteMetrics` (`dispatch_route_metrics.go`) | None | `system-rib:route-metrics` |
| `inject-wire-route` | `opInjectWireRoute` (`dispatch_registry.go`) | `RequirePeerDeclarations` only | `bgp:raw` |
| `resolve-dns` | `opResolveDNS` (`dispatch_resolve.go`) | None | A message the resolver registers (name owed at implementation) |
| `batch-validate` | `opBatchValidate`, `dispatchBatchValidation` (`dispatch_batch_validate.go`) | Dispatches `rpc.BatchValidateCommand` (`request bgp adj-rib-in batch-validate`) through `dispatchCommandArgs` on both transports, so the prefix bypass | `cli:request bgp adj-rib-in batch-validate` (Q-T3, option (a)); legacy test-only `DirectBridge.SetBatchValidate` removed or gated |
| `ze-bgp:source-lease` (owner-registered) | `sourceLeaseJSON` (`bgp/reactor/register_source_lease.go`) | `sourceLeaseAdmission`: requires `proc.SelectedOwner()`, matching `PeerOwner`, and (for `acquire`/`retain` only) peer dissemination receive grant; `release` stays callable during teardown | `none`: caller's own source-receipt leases, gated by `SelectedOwner` and peer dissemination receive grant; joins `EngineOperation` walk (Q-T1, option (a)) |
| `ze-firewall:tables-replace` (owner-registered) | `tablesReplaceHandler` (`firewall/register_contribution.go`) | `refuseOwnerWithoutReservation(proc.Name())` and `refuseUnreservedContribution`, keyed on the operator's alias today | `none`: governed by compile-time `firewall.MustReserveTableNames` grants, moved to compiled registration identity (Q-T1, Q-T2) |
| `ze-sysrib:route-view-open`, `route-view-read`, `route-view-close`, `route-view-candidates`, `route-view-distances`, `route-view-publish` (owner-registered) | `sysrib/register_route_view*.go` | All six call `routeViewAuthorize(owner)` requiring `RegistrationName() == "rib"`; the five post-open ops additionally enforce exact `*process.Process` + session token via `authority.match` | `none`: exclusive daemon-RIB session protocol gated by `RegistrationName() == "rib"` and live session token; joins walk (Q-T1, option (a)) |
| `ze-iface:owned-bridges-replace`, `owned-link-create`, `owned-link-remove` (owner-registered) | `iface/cmd/owned_devices.go` | `iface.RefuseUngrantedDeviceOwner(proc.Name())`, keyed on the operator's alias today | `none`: governed by compile-time `iface.MustGrantOwnedDeviceNames` prefix grants, moved to compiled registration identity (Q-T1, Q-T2) |

The owner-registered rows hold eleven operations, registered through `RegisterEngineOp` from six files: `bgp/reactor/register_source_lease.go` (1), `firewall/register_contribution.go` (1), `sysrib/register_route_view.go` (3: open, read, close), `sysrib/register_route_view_policy.go` (2: candidates, distances), `sysrib/register_route_view_output.go` (1: publish) and `iface/cmd/owned_devices.go` (3), read 2026-11-23. The census report counted 12; the six registration files hold 11.

How the authorization bypass closes. Today `dispatchCommandPeerResponse` and `dispatchCommandArgsResponse` set the command's username to `internalPluginIdentity(proc.Name())`, which carries `aaa.ReservedInternalPrefix`, and `Store.authorize` (`internal/component/authz/authz.go`) returns `Allow` for that prefix before it looks at any profile.

| Option | What it does | Verdict |
|--------|--------------|---------|
| (a) A profile for the plugin identity | The participant's filter for `cli` messages (declared, cut by its send allow-list and its `to`) is compiled into an ordinary authorization profile bound to the plugin identity. `Store.authorize` stops allowing the `plugin:` identities by prefix and evaluates that profile with the matcher it uses for every user. Accounting and audit keep the identity | Chosen: one authorizer, one matcher, one audit trail |
| (b) A second gate in `plugin/server` before the dispatcher | The plugin server checks the path, then dispatches under the reserved identity as today | Set aside: two authorizers for one command, and the prefix bypass stays in `authz` for any path that forgets the first gate |
| (c) Keep the bypass for compiled-in plugins | Only programs Ze did not ship are checked | Set aside: S-1 rules out a trust difference |

`internalRPCIdentity`, the reserved identity of an in-process RPC call that carries no plugin, stays allowed, with its reason restated on the constant: it names no participant, so no filter exists to check it against. Each of its callers is listed at implementation.

One refusal, one counter, one doctor code:

| Existing mechanism | Where | Here |
|--------------------|-------|------|
| Request refusal event | `plugin/server/dispatch_report.go` (`refusePluginRequest`, `diagnostic.CodeDoctorPluginRequestRefused`) | Extends: a message refused by the filter raises it, with the participant, the message and the fix |
| Send refusal counter | `bgp/reactor/send_permission.go` (`recordSendRefused`, metric `ze_bgp_send_refused_total`) | Replaces: one counter in the plugin server for every operation, labels participant and message. The BGP counter is deleted, its page updated |
| Send refusal text | `send_permission.go` (`sendPermissionDenied`, `errSendNotPermitted`) | Extends: one text shape for every operation, saying which reason applies |

| Reason a message is refused | Message the operator reads |
|-----------------------------|----------------------------|
| The participant's kind did not declare it | Names the participant and the message, says its registration does not declare it, and gives the command that shows what it does declare. No config line fixes this |
| An allow-list removed it | Names the participant and the list, and gives the exact line that adds the entry |
| A `to` or `from` list does not cover the other end | Names both participants and the line that holds the list |

### The display: nothing implied is hidden

One pipe operation, `| implied`, shows every registered default beside what the operator wrote, with what produced it. Every implied kind comes from a registration; none is computed from a name in engine or config code. The view reads the one compiled filter per participant, so it cannot differ from what runs.

| Shown item | Produced by | What the view prints |
|------------|-------------|----------------------|
| An endpoint Ze loads with no `endpoint` written | The plugin's `ConfigRoots`, `Families`, `Dependencies`, `ConfigNeeds`, `DataPlane` | The endpoint, with the config path, family or dependent plugin that implies it |
| A participant's effective `to` and `from`, per message | The registration, a group's block, the participant's block, or the bus side | Each effective entry with the line that produced it: written here, inherited (naming the group), registered, required, or dormant |
| The defaults a written list replaces | The registration | Beside a written `to`, the registered destinations it replaced, so the operator sees what the list removed |
| The effective send and receive allow-lists | The registration and the two places | The declared names, and which a written list keeps. A present and empty list is shown as written, distinct from an absent one |
| A filter member Ze inserted in a peer's filter list | The one implied-filter seam (core-design decision of 2026-11-23): the plugin's `FilterInsertions` and the built-in BGP engine steps (`next-hop:auto` at the export tail, `loop-detection` at the import head), each with direction, position, reference and `implied-by` reason | Every extra chain member beside the operator's written filter list, with the config path or the rule that inserted it. The view reads the seam and names no member itself |
| A delivery a feature requires | The feature's declared requirement | Marked required, with the feature |

| Surface | Form |
|---------|------|
| Pipe, bare, no argument | `show config running \| implied`, `show config source <file> \| implied`, `show config dump <file> \| implied`, editor `show \| implied` and `show \| implied \| format config`. Written and implied items together; standard pipes such as `\| match` filter them |
| Text | A comment `# implied by: <reason>` above each whole implied block, and an inline `# implied: <what was added and why>` on the same line when `\| implied` appends to an existing config statement (such as a `filters [ ... ];` line, per `docs/architecture/api/architecture.md` Design decision of 2026-11-23): always a single hash followed by a space, never `##` at column 0, which `config.DetectFormat` (`internal/component/config/serialize_set.go`) reads as a set-meta line. An implied `to` is printed as the `bus` block that would state it, in the short path form, so an operator can copy it and change it |
| JSON and YAML | An envelope with the written config under `config` and the implied items under `implied`, each with `implied-by`. The written config stays byte-pure |
| Plain-display notice | When implied items exist, a human display states the count and names `\| implied`: the editor status bar, the `\| text` footer, `stderr` of `ze config show`. Never in `\| json`, `\| yaml`, REST, gRPC or `ze config show` stdout |
| Web | The web CLI and the graphical inspection offer `show \| implied`. Workbench tree pages show implied rows read-only, with a badge |
| gNMI | `Get` returns the written config only, so `Get` then `Set` round-trips |
| The whole bus | `show event delivery` lists every namespace, each edge once with its origin (`written`, `inherited`, `registered`, `required`, `dormant`), naming both participants by path; `ze config graph` draws the same edges. `required` replaces `ProcessEdges.Implicit` |
| No daemon, external endpoint | The view takes the program's declaration in query mode. When the program cannot be asked, the view refuses, names the endpoint, its `run` line and the cause, and gives `ze show plugin conformance run "<run line>"`. It never shows a partial set as complete |

| Rule | Statement (carried from the absorbed `spec-bus-2`, restated on the message model) |
|------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Where `\| implied` lives | Read at the producers on 2026-11-23: `internal/component/command/pipe_catalog.go` holds the generic operators that act on any command's answer by its shape (formats, paging, `save`, `match`, `count`, and the rest), while `internal/component/command/pipe_filter.go` holds the operations a command declares for itself (`PipeFilter`, `RegisterPipeFilters`, with an argument kind such as `ArgNone`) and its own handler runs. `\| implied` is computed from the compiled filters by the config view commands and means nothing for another command's answer, so it is a command-declared operation: the three config view commands (`show config running`, `show config source`, `show config dump`) register `implied` through `RegisterPipeFilters` with `ArgNone`. Its name is no generic's, so it is not an override. `pipe_catalog.go` is not changed. The editor's `show \| implied` is classified by `ClassifyShowPipes` (`internal/component/cli/model_load.go`), which both the SSH and the web CLI call |
| The JSON and YAML envelope | `config` holds the written configuration, byte-pure. `implied` holds one list per implied kind: `endpoints` (implied endpoints, each with `implied-by`), `participants` (per participant path: the effective `send` lines, `allow`, `to` and per-message `to` and `tag`, and `receive` lines, `allow`, `from` and per-message `from` and `tag`, each with its `origin` and producing line, and the registered destinations a written `to` replaced), and `filters` (filter members a plugin inserted). The absorbed spec's `flows` and `sends` keys are superseded by `participants`. Keys are kebab-case (`docs/architecture/api/json-format.md`) |
| The round trip | An implied item is printed as the `bus` block that would state it, in the stored short path form. Pasted back as written configuration, it commits, and is then shown once, as written, with no implied copy beside it (S30) |
| One registration, one line | A participant whose wiring comes from its registration is shown once, under its own path, never once per peer it serves: the route server with no list written shows its `bgp:update` send once, as registered, and lists every connected peer as a participant its registered destinations cover (S29) |
| A dynamic peer | A peer admitted by a dynamic group's range is shown, on `show event delivery` and in `\| implied`, with the path of the group that admitted it, beside its address (S31). A line naming one dynamic peer by its address (M-18) is shown dormant until a peer with that address connects, then as that peer's effective entry (S22) |
| Lifecycle deliveries | The deliveries that bypass the per-peer grant today are registered receptions and appear like any other: `SubscribedProcsNamed` (`plugin/server/delivery_graph.go`), which tells a process that a peer stopped feeding it, and `addOpenRemovalObservers` (`bgp/server/events.go`), which hands a peer's removal to every OPEN validator that declared `WantsValidateOpen` without a peer attachment. Each becomes a reception the validator's or the process's registration declares, filtered and shown with origin `registered` (S32) |
| Auto-loaded endpoints | Each loader of `startup_autoload.go` returns its reason as a typed value (kind + producing config path, family or dependency), and that report is the implied endpoint list: `Server.getConfigPathPlugins`, `Server.getUnclaimedFamilyPlugins`, `Server.resolveAutoLoad` and `Server.appendDataPlaneWriter`. `Server.getUnclaimedEventTypePlugins` and `Server.getUnclaimedSendTypePlugins` are deleted (Q-11, option (c)): verified at the producers that only two plugins set `EventTypes`/`SendTypes` (`bgp-route-refresh` already declares `ConfigRoots: ["bgp"]`, and `bgp-rpki-decorator` has only one `.ci` test relying on token auto-load, routed through the test judge). A reception whose sender is not loaded is dormant (M-4), and `\| implied` and `show doctor` name the plugin that sends it and the `bus { endpoint ... }` line that loads it |

### Feature requirements

Today BGP config code names plugins to decide what a feature needs: `requireRIBDelivery` (`internal/component/bgp/config/flowspec_binding.go`) holds the literals `bgp-rs`, `bgp-rr` and `bgp-adj-rib-in`, and `wireRedistributeDelivery` and `requireSelectionFeeds` (`redistribute_binding.go`) reach the Loc-RIB and the orchestrator through named constants.

| Step | Design |
|------|--------|
| The declaration | A plugin declares, on its registration, each requirement a feature's config places on it: the condition (a config path or a session fact, in the form `FilterInsertions` conditions already use) and the messages that condition needs to pass between which participants. It generalizes `rpc.DisseminationDecl` |
| The check | One function compares every active requirement with the compiled filters, at validate, commit, reload and start, with one verdict |
| The refusal | Where a `to`, a `from` or an allow-list removes something an active requirement needs, the commit is refused. The message names the feature, the participant, the line that removes it and the line that completes it, as a typed `FixCommand`. Ze never widens what was written and never loads it to do nothing |
| What it replaces | The literals in `requireRIBDelivery`, the constants behind `wireRedistributeDelivery` and `requireSelectionFeeds`, the derived bindings (`grantEveryPeer`, `EnsureProcessBinding`), and the per-role check of `ValidateDisseminationBindings` with its `disseminationAttachLine` |
| Redistribution | Gains the refusal it lacks today: a rule whose Loc-RIB or orchestrator is cut below what the rule needs is refused, where today it loads and moves no route with one log line |
| The filter owner's `state` | The owner of a filter member requires the peer's `state`. The requirement declaration states it, in place of `grantFilterOwnersState` naming it in BGP config code. An override that removes it is refused like any other requirement, where today the grant silently widens the operator's block (`GrantImplicitReceive`) |
| AIGP | `PeerSettings.AIGPEnabled` (`bgp/reactor/config_aigp.go`) answers true for every iBGP peer with no `aigp` configured (RFC 7311 Section 3.3). A capability that is merely on by default implies nothing (S-8): only a peer with `aigp` written activates the requirement. The session default, and what Ze sends, accepts and compares, are unchanged. A test on the default configuration proves a route carrying AIGP is still compared by AIGP |
| Dynamic groups | The collectors in `bgp/config/loader_create.go` that skip dynamic groups for family, event and send auto-load are fixed, so an implied endpoint a dynamic group needs is loaded and shown |

### No arbitration: route server and reflector

Under M-11 the bus picks no winner, so two plugins that both forward must not duplicate each other by default. Read at the producers on 2026-11-23:

| Producer | What it does today | Under the default wiring |
|----------|--------------------|--------------------------|
| `peerStateUnheldRoles` (`bgp/server/events.go`) | The role `bgp-peer-up-replay` is held for a peer when a fed claimant may send `update` to it (`graph.MaySend`) | Same question, answered by the compiled filter. Held by both claimants when both are loaded |
| `rsRelaySendProcessName` (`bgp/reactor/peer_forward_facts.go`) | The first process in the peer's bindings that may send `update` and whose plugin declares `registry.RelaysOnRSFastPath` | Reads the filter. Only `bgp-rs` declares the fast path |
| `Peer.policyReplayOwner` (`bgp/reactor/policy_reload.go`) | The first binding that may send `update`, receives the peer's state and claims `bgp-peer-up-replay`; else the store that owns the replay command | Reads the filter. With two claimants on one peer it returns the first in order: a silent winner |
| `Peer.initialUpdateReporters` (`bgp/reactor/peer_run.go`) | Every process with a route-push rail on the peer, the peer's state and `SignalsSessionReady` | Every such plugin, on every peer it serves |
| `routeServer.selectForwardTargets` (`bgp/plugins/rs/server_forward.go`), `PeerState.updateTarget` (`rs/peer.go`) | Targets every peer the route server knows that is up and not send-denied (`Up && !updateSendDenied`). No session-type condition in the selection | Every peer it is fed |
| `bgp-rs` and `bgp-rr` registrations (`rs/register.go`, `rr/register.go`) | Both claim `bgp-peer-up-replay` and both set `SignalsSessionReady` and `FencesLiveForwards` | Both are fed every peer and may send to every peer |

Finding: with `bgp-rs` and `bgp-rr` both loaded and nothing written, both are fed every peer and both hold `update` toward every peer, so both would forward every received route to every other peer and both would replay at peer-up, and one peer would receive the same routes twice. Today the per-peer `attach process` blocks keep them apart. Verified at the producers on 2026-11-24 (A-10): neither `rs` (`handleStateUp`, `handleUpWithoutReplay`, `handleOpen`, `selectForwardTargets`) nor `rr` (`handleStructuredState`, `recordOpen`, `targets`) checks `rs-client` or iBGP/eBGP today; the reactor's `forwardUpdateCore` (`reactor_api_forward.go:882-888`) and `reactorForwardRS` (`forward_rs.go:460-465`) enforce RFC 4456 reflection between iBGP peers, while allowing eBGP sources to feed iBGP RR clients (`bgp-policy-diagnostics-internal.ci`). This is a defect of those two plugins under the new default, fixed in this work (Q-5, option (a)): each plugin limits its forwarding targets by the session it is for. The route server serves the peers of its client set (`session rs-client`; `ze-rs-conf.yang`: "A peer joins the client set with the rs-client leaf of its session", RFC 7947 Section 1), and the reflector targets internal peers (`!isEBGP`, RFC 4456 Section 5-6) while still accepting eBGP sources to distribute eBGP routes to its internal clients. `policyReplayOwner` and the role retraction then find one claimant per peer because their target sets are disjoint (`rs-client` is eBGP, RR targets are iBGP), with no arbitration in the bus. Census of `test/plugin/`: 101 of 158 `.ci` files configuring `bgp-rs` omit `rs-client`; those split into true RS tests (which gain `session { rs-client true; }`) and non-RS eBGP transit tests (which move to `bgp-rib`), routed through the test judge (R-14).

| Role | The RFC's words | What follows |
|------|-----------------|--------------|
| Route server | RFC 7947 Section 1: "Each multilateral interconnection participant (usually referred to as a "route server client") announces network reachability information to the route server using EBGP. The route server, in turn, forwards this information to each route server client connected to it, according to its configuration." | The route server serves every route server client connected to it. A difference between clients is export policy in the peer's filter list |
| Route reflector | RFC 4456 Section 5: "The internal peers of an RR are divided into two groups: 1) Client peers 2) Non-Client peers". Section 6: a route from a non-client is reflected "to all the Clients", and a route from a client "to all the Non-Client peers and also to the Client peers" | Client or non-client is BGP configuration of the peer. It changes the reflection rule, never whether the plugin may talk to the peer |

An operator who wants a forwarder kept to some peers writes a `send > to` on it (`bgp { route-server { bus { send { to [ /bgp/group/ix ]; } } } }`), or a `receive > from` on the peers.

### ExaBGP

Owner decision E-1: ExaBGP compatibility is a normal plugin, the `exabgp { bridge }` section and the internal bridge mode are removed. The investigation behind this section (2026-11-23 and 2026-11-24) read `docs/architecture/exabgp-bridge.md`, `docs/exabgp/exabgp-migration.md`, `internal/plugins/exabgp/main.go`, `main_sdk.go`, `bridgerun/fleet.go`, `bridgerun/script.go`, `bridgeplugin/config.go`, `internal/exabgp/bridge/bridge_owner.go`, `bridge_event.go`, `bridge_negotiated.go`, `bridge_command.go`, `bridge_selector.go`, `bridge_watchdog.go`, `internal/component/bgp/plugins/watchdog/given.go`, `internal/component/plugin/registration.go`, `internal/component/plugin/process/process.go`, `internal/component/plugin/ipc/plugin_name.go` and `internal/exabgp/migration/migrate.go`. Every statement below was read at the producer it names. Thomas answered Q-E1 (E-2), Q-E2 (E-5), Q-E5 (E-4), Q-E6 (E-3), Q-E8 and standalone exit (E-6) and Q-E10 (E-7); Q-E3, Q-E4, Q-E9 and Q-E11 carry the main session's producer-verified recommendations made while Thomas was AFK (recorded under "Choices for Thomas" for his review); Q-E7 remains an owed measurement on a known host.

**Three runners today, two after** (E-1, E-3: the endpoint form and the standalone form).

| Runner | Entry | Where its scripts come from | What it does today |
|--------|-------|-----------------------------|--------------------|
| Compiled-in bridge | `bridgeplugin.runInternalBridge`, config root `exabgp { bridge { process <name> { ... } } }` (`ze-exabgp-bridge-conf.yang`) | Every `process` block, parsed at Stage 2 (`parseScripts`, `parseFeeds`, `parseEncoder`, `parseOnExit`) | One `bridgerun.Fleet` of every script; `bridgeRegistration` declares `FailurePolicy: sdk.FailureRestart` |
| Wrapper over TLS | `exabgp.runSDKMode` (`main_sdk.go`), chosen by `cmdPlugin` when `ZE_PLUGIN_HUB_TOKEN` is set | Its command line: one script | A `bridgerun.Fleet` of that one script, `Encoder: bridge.EncoderJSON`, `Respawn: true`, no feed, `Name: filepath.Base(pluginCmd[0])`; it authenticates as `sdk.NewFromTLSEnv(bridge.PluginName)`, the fixed name `exabgp-bridge`; its `sdk.Registration` holds `Families` and `WantsConfig: bgp` and no failure policy; it subscribes `*` (`SetStartupSubscriptions`) |
| Standalone | `bridge.Bridge.Run` (`main.go`, no hub token) | Its command line: one script | MuxConn on its own stdin and stdout, no Fleet, no respawn; answers `status` with `error` (`errStatusStandalone`). Kept by E-3, as a help for users testing a script by hand; its help text is repaired with the wrapper's. Whether any test runs it today is unverified, so a functional test of it is owed (`exabgp-standalone-runner`) |

After: each ExaBGP script is one endpoint, `bus { endpoint <name> { external { run "ze exabgp plugin [options] <script>"; } } }`, and the standalone form stays for a script run by hand (E-3). Deleted first, with no layering: `internal/plugins/exabgp/bridgeplugin/` and its five test files, `ze-exabgp-bridge-conf.yang`, the `exabgp` config root with its `family`, `route-refresh` and `add-path` leaves (E-4), the `feed` grammar, and `bridgePluginName` and `injectBridgePlugin` in the migration.

**Carried unchanged inside the wrapper.** `runSDKMode` already builds a `bridgerun.Fleet` of one script, so each of these runs in the wrapper as it runs in the internal bridge:

| Behavior | Producer |
|----------|----------|
| Translation in both directions, the batch and its netting, the group, the per-script ack | `script.batch` through `bridge.ScriptLines.Batch` |
| The API version a script is held to | `bridge.ScriptVersion`, one per script (`newScript`) |
| The fork limit: five forks in one 64-second window | `respawnLimiter`, `respawnMax = 5`, `respawnWindow = 64 * time.Second` (`bridgerun/respawn.go`), asked by `script.supervise`. Not carried (E-6): the translator restarts nothing, so `respawn.go`, the supervise loop and `--respawn` are deleted; the endpoint's restart is the process manager's, whose limit is five restarts in a 60-second window and twenty per daemon life before the endpoint is disabled (`RespawnLimit`, `RespawnWindow`, `MaxTotalRespawns`, `ErrProcessDisabled`, `process/manager.go`, read 2026-11-23). That limit differs from ExaBGP's 64-second bucket and has no lifetime cap in ExaBGP: a documented difference |
| Withdrawal of an exited script's routes, with `restore-configured`, and the watchdog forget before it | `OwnerLedger.Exited`, `restoringWithdrawal`, `watchdogForgetCommand`, called from `script.exited`. Not carried in the wrapper (E-6): the translator exits with its script, so the engine's withdrawal by originator (E-2) is the only route-withdrawal mechanism and `OwnerLedger` with `--on-exit` is deleted. The watchdog forget step stays (Q-E11, option (a)): verified in `bgp/plugins/watchdog/given.go` that `bgp-watchdog` stores given routes in its own `s.given` map and announces them under `bgp-watchdog`'s own connection, so `EndOriginOwner` alone cannot see or withdraw them. On participant exit without `external { on-exit keep; }`, the bus emits a generic participant-exit lifecycle message that `bgp-watchdog` registers to receive and handles via `handleForget(endpointName)` |
| ExaBGP's shutdown line before the script's stdin closes | `Fleet.Shutdown`, which `runSDKMode` calls after `p.Run` returns |
| The `status` answer from ze's peer list | `script.status` through `scriptRunner` |
| The event format, `full` or `parsed` | `bridgerun.EventFormat` over the one script |
| The negotiated block per grant | `Fleet.Broadcast`, `Event.ForGrant` |

**What the wrapper changes.**

| Point | Today (producer) | After |
|-------|------------------|-------|
| Identity | `sdk.NewFromTLSEnv(bridge.PluginName)` fixes `exabgp-bridge`, while the process manager issues a token bound to the configured name | The wrapper authenticates as its endpoint name, and names its script by the endpoint name, which keys the log lines, the owner ledger and the watchdog forget (today `filepath.Base(pluginCmd[0])`) |
| Failure policy | None in `runSDKMode`'s registration | `FailurePolicy` restart, as `bridgeRegistration` declares today. The wrapper exits when its script exits, with the script's outcome (E-6); the endpoint's `external { respawn }` restarts both, and `ze exabgp migrate` writes it from the ExaBGP process's `respawn` setting. `exabgp-bridge.md`, "A script that exits is started again", is rewritten in the same work |
| Declarations | Subscribes every event (`*`) and declares no send | Declares the reception of every BGP event the translator renders and the send of every command path it emits, derived from one event table and one command table added in `internal/exabgp/bridge/` (verified 2026-11-24 that neither table exists yet: events are spread across `bridge_event.go`/`bridge_event_fsm.go` and 16 emitted CLI command prefixes across 7 files) |
| Scope | No peer list: `Translator.Peers` is nil, so an unaddressed line goes to `*`, and every peer feeds every event | Derived from the wrapper's own compiled filter, handed generically at Stage 2 and at each rebuild (Q-E3, option (a)): `Translator.Peers`, the per-peer grant set and the event format. Verified in `bridge_command.go:345` (`netip.ParseAddr`) and `bridgerun/script.go:165` (`read.Peer`): the handed filter MUST carry each resolved peer's remote IP address beside its path |
| Event format written to the script | `Encoder: bridge.EncoderJSON`, fixed | `--encoder text\|json`, default `text`, the default `parseEncoder` reads for an absent leaf. `test/plugin/exabgp-bridge-sdk.ci` and `exabgp-bridge-shutdown-external.ci` state `--encoder json`. The endpoint's `encoding` leaf is the engine-to-wrapper format and stays `json`, because `Fleet.Broadcast` unmarshals each event as JSON; it never carries the script's format |
| Respawn | `Respawn: true`, fixed | Deleted with `--respawn` (E-6): the endpoint's `external { respawn }` is the only restart |
| On exit | Unset | Deleted with `--on-exit` (E-6): it reduces to `external { on-exit keep; }` (Q-E11, option (a); default `withdraw`), which `ze exabgp migrate` writes for `on-exit keep` and for a process held to API version 4 with no `on-exit`. A script that moves its own API version at run time no longer changes what its exit does: a documented difference |
| Forms | `bridge.FormParsed` for a script with no grants (`script.forms`) | `--form [<neighbor>:]<direction>-<parsed\|packets\|consolidate>`, repeatable, the tokens `bridge.FormsFor` reads (`receive-packets` and its siblings) |
| Family and capabilities | `--family`, `--route-refresh`, `--add-path` (`cmdPlugin`, `main.go`); `runSDKMode` builds `FamilyDecl` entries and `CapabilityDecl` entries (code 2, and code 69 from `bridge.EncodeAddPathHex`) and declares them through `p.DeclareCapabilitiesFrom`, ignoring the config sections | The three options stay, unchanged, as the translator's one source: it declares what they state as the capabilities of its virtual session at Stage 3 ("Virtual session capabilities", E-4). `ze exabgp migrate` writes them on the `run` line from the ExaBGP configuration (E-7, "The migration, after") |
| Help text | `usage` and `cmdPlugin` show `plugin exabgp-compat { run ...; }`; `cmdMigrate` says `process -> plugin` "Wrapped with ze exabgp plugin bridge" and `api { processes [...] } -> attach process NAME`; `MigrateFromExaBGP`'s comment says the same. All stale today: the migration writes the bridge root | Repaired in the same work to the endpoint form |

**Scope is written once, in the endpoint's `bus` block.**

| ExaBGP relation | Today (`feed`, `grantSet`, `feedPeers`) | After |
|-----------------|------------------------------------------|-------|
| Which neighbor feeds which message kind | `feed <address> { event [ ... ]; }` | `receive { from [ ... ]; }` names the neighbors that grant any kind; `receive { allow [ ... ]; }` holds the union of the kinds; a `receive { message <kind> { from [ ... ]; } }` is written only where neighbors grant different kinds |
| Where an unaddressed command goes | `feedPeers`: every peer with a feed block, one that grants no event included (ExaBGP's `Reactor.peers(service)` reads `api['processes']` alone) | `send { to [ ... ]; }`: the neighbors that name the script |
| A neighbor that names a script and no kind | A feed block with an empty `event` list | In `send > to`, outside a written `receive > from`; `receive { from none; }` when every neighbor naming the script is like that (Q-1) |
| A process no neighbor names, or one a ze-native `process` binding names with no selection | No feed block: every peer, every event (`scriptFeeds.write` skips an unscoped process) | No `bus` block: the registered wiring |
| `parsed`, `packets`, `consolidate` | Entries of the feed's `event` list (`appendFormGrants`) | One `--form` per granted form word |

**ExaBGP API key to bus message mapping (Q-E4, option (a), verified 2026-11-24):** the bus filters per-neighbor message kinds using `receive > from`, `receive > allow`, `receive > message <name> > from` and direction tags (`received` / `sent`):

| ExaBGP `api` key | Ze bus message and direction tag | Notes |
|------------------|----------------------------------|-------|
| `neighbor-changes` | `bgp:state` and `bgp:connected` | `bridge.ReadEvent` folds `connected` into `state` |
| `negotiated` | `bgp:negotiated` | Also controls attaching the `negotiated` block via `Event.ForGrant` |
| `fsm` | `bgp:fsm` | Unchanged |
| `signal` | `bgp:signal` | Unchanged |
| `receive-open` / `send-open` | `bgp:open` with tag `direction [ received ]` / `[ sent ]` | `bridge.Sessions` does not read `open` |
| `receive-update` / `send-update` | `bgp:update` with tag `direction [ received ]` / `[ sent ]` | Includes received EOR |
| `receive-notification` / `send-notification` | `bgp:notification` with tag `direction [ received ]` / `[ sent ]` | Unchanged |
| `receive-keepalive` / `send-keepalive` | `bgp:keepalive` with tag `direction [ received ]` / `[ sent ]` | Unchanged |
| `receive-refresh` / `send-refresh` | `bgp:refresh` with tag `direction [ received ]` / `[ sent ]` | Covers `refresh`, `borr`, `eorr` |
| `receive-operational` / `send-operational` | *(none)* | Unsupported by Ze (`ai/META-DECISIONS.md`) |

Two `bridge.Sessions` invariants verified in `bridge_negotiated.go` and `bridge_event_wire.go` keep Option (a) lossless when `bgp:negotiated` or `bgp:state` is excluded from `receive > allow`: (1) `bgp:update` events carry the peer's 4-byte ASN width (`context_id` / `as4`) directly so `overlayASPath` does not depend on having received `bgp:negotiated`; and (2) `bridge.Sessions` keys each cached `negotiated` block by `(peer, connectionID)` so a reconnected session never reuses a stale `negotiated` block even without `bgp:state`.

The wrapper addresses its `send > to` and never `*`: `filterPermittedPeers` (`reactor/send_permission.go`) counts each peer it drops as a refusal and `sendPermissionDenied` logs a WARN for each, so a `*` narrowed by the bus would log a refusal per peer outside `to`. Verified in `bridge_command.go:339-342` and `bridge_selector.go:196-198`: ExaBGP's `match_neighbors` matches an explicit `neighbor *` / `peer *` ONLY against `Reactor.peers(service)`, whereas Ze's `Translator.servedSelector` short-circuits `selector == "*"` even when `len(t.Peers) > 0`; this work fixes `servedSelector` to return `t.everyPeer(), false` when `selector == "*" && len(t.Peers) > 0`.

How a script's route reaches a peer is stated by the rule rows of "The `bus` block on a participant": verified in `bridgerun/script.go:377-380` that the ExaBGP translator dispatches translated route lines (`send bgp <sel> update text ...`) via `dispatch-command` (not `update-route`). Under Q-T5 option (a), peer-selecting `cli` commands check `send > to` against the peers the command selects (`filterPermittedPeers`) and check the `cli:` prefix (`"cli:send bgp"` — `Store.authorize` sees the inline peer selector at token 3 before `update`) against `send > allow`. AC-33 and S44 follow this rule.

**The migration, after.**

| Point | Today (producer) | After |
|-------|------------------|-------|
| A process | `injectBridgePlugin` writes `exabgp { bridge { process <name> { run; respawn; encoder; on-exit } } }` and a `plugin` entry `exabgp-bridge` using `exabgp-bridge` | One endpoint per process, its `run` the wrapper with the options the block stated (`--family`, `--route-refresh`, `--add-path` from the ExaBGP config per E-7, `--encoder`, `--form`) and nothing for a default. The process's `respawn` becomes the endpoint's `external { respawn }`, written only as `false`, because a restart is both ExaBGP's default (`exabgpRespawn`) and what the wrapper's `FailurePolicy` restart asks. Its `on-exit` becomes `external { on-exit keep; }` (Q-E11, option (a)) for `on-exit keep`, and for a process held to API version 4 with no `on-exit`; `on-exit withdraw` writes nothing, withdrawal being the default (E-2, E-6). No `--respawn` or `--on-exit` option is written: the wrapper has none |
