| show event delivery answer | reader | Operators and scripts reading `\| json`; `test/plugin/redistribute-explicit-attach-kept.ci`; `docs/architecture/api/architecture.md` | grep of `show event delivery` under `test/` and `docs/architecture/` in this pass | Converted in the same work with the answer; the `.ci` is restated through the test judge where it proves the old binding |
| Stage 1 dissemination declaration | writer | `rpc.DisseminationDecl` in `pkg/plugin/rpc/types.go`; `disseminationDecls` in `internal/component/bgp/plugins/rs/dissemination.go`, `rr/dissemination.go`, `rib/rib_dissemination.go`; a third-party plugin in any language | grep of `DisseminationDecl` in this pass | Changed directly into the feature requirement declaration ("Feature requirements"); every plugin in the tree rebuilt, no version gate |
| Stage 3 declare-capabilities | writer | `pkg/plugin/sdk` (`DeclareCapabilitiesFrom`); the ExaBGP wrapper (`main_sdk.go` `runSDKMode`); every capability plugin; a third-party plugin in any language | `rpc.DeclareCapabilitiesInput`, a list of `rpc.CapabilityDecl` (`Code`, `Encoding`, `Payload`, `Peers`), read in this pass | Shape unchanged. What a declaration means changes: the capabilities of the declaring participant's virtual session (E-4) |
| Stage 3 declare-capabilities | reader | `plugin/server/startup.go` (`engineStartupSink.onCapabilities`); `plugin/server/config_tx_capabilities.go` (`stageVerifiedCapabilities`); `plugin/registration.go` (`CapabilityInjector.AddPluginCapabilities`, `CheckReplacePluginCapabilities`, `planLocked`); `bgp/reactor/session_negotiate.go` (`buildOpenFromSnapshot`) | `planLocked` refuses a global code another plugin registered: "capability conflict: code %d already registered by %s", read in this pass | Changed in the same work: the same code and identical payload bytes from two participants deduplicates to one capability on the wire with no conflict; different values toward one peer are refused at start and at verify naming both endpoints and the fix (Q-E9, option (a)) |
| Stage 1 dissemination declaration | reader | `internal/component/plugin/server/dissemination_source.go` and `dissemination_validate.go` (`registry.CheckDisseminationDeclarations`, `ValidateDisseminationBindings`) | grep of `DisseminationDecl` in this pass | Replaced by the one requirement check against the compiled filters |

### Integration Points
- `internal/component/plugin/registry/registry.go` (`Registration`) - the participant declarations replace `EventTypes` and `SendTypes`; a declared feature requirement generalizes `Dissemination`.
- `internal/core/events/events.go` - the message registry.
- `internal/component/bgp/config/resolve.go` (`ResolveBGPTree`) - the replace-whole marker for the bus lists.
- `internal/component/config/yang/join.go` (`FirstPassRoots`) - derived from a declaration on the root.
- `internal/component/plugin/server/delivery_graph.go` (`DeliveryGraph`) - built from the compiled filters.
- `internal/component/plugin/server/dispatch_registry.go`, `register_engine_op.go` - the message field and the one check.
- `internal/component/authz/authz.go` (`Store.authorize`) - the profile of a plugin identity.

### Architectural Verification
| Check | Holds? | Evidence |
|-------|--------|----------|
| No bypassed layers (data flows through the intended path) | Yes | One compiled filter per participant feeds delivery, the engine-operation check, the authorization profile, the display and the reconcile |
| No unintended coupling (components stay isolated) | Yes | The bus carries no BGP word; participant nodes and messages are registered by their owners; `internal/core/events` stays a leaf |
| No duplicated functionality (extends existing, does not recreate) | Yes | Every row of Existing Mechanisms is extended or deleted; no second authorizer, no second inheritance, no second dispatch path |
| Zero-copy preserved where applicable (refs, not copies) | Yes | UPDATE buffers and `RawMessage` lifetime are untouched; the index stores receivers per sender and message, and the benchmarks assert no allocation |
| Registration over hardcoding, outbound: new commands, views, families, and handlers register, and the core discovers them. No per-feature field, switch case, or factory is added to a core/shared package (`ai/rules/plugins.md`) | Yes | Participants, messages and feature requirements register from `init()`; the bus validates from those registries |
| Registration over hardcoding, inbound: no existing switch, seed map, validator, parser, runner, help string, or completion table has to learn this feature's name. Evidence names every list that was searched for the names this feature introduces, and the registry each one now derives from (`ai/rules/principles.md`) | Yes, once built | `firstPassRoots` (`config/yang/join.go`) is a literal list that would learn `bus`; this design derives it. `replacePaths` would learn the bus lists; this design reads a schema marker. The three plugin-name literals of `requireRIBDelivery` are replaced by the declared requirement. `validatedSections`, `retiredKeywords` and `listenerSectionName` were searched |

## Risks & Assumptions

### Assumptions
| ID | Assumption | Basis (file/doc/user statement) | If wrong | Validated by | Status |
|----|-----------|--------------------------------|----------|--------------|--------|
| A-1 | Dropping `attach > content` removes no working feature | `delivery_graph.go` (`addBinding`) drops both fields; `bgp/server/events.go` reads only the process's encoding | A consumer depends on per-attachment encoding | Repo-wide grep for `ProcessBinding` readers | validated |
| A-2 | No repository test depends on a program not seeing a peer, apart from the tests that prove that silence itself | The receive census (2026-11-23) | A scenario's result changes because a program now receives a peer's events | The receive census | measured: holds only with the listed tests routed to the judge |
| A-3 | The messages a compiled-in plugin receives are a constant of the plugin, not a function of its config | Verified 2026-11-24 across all 21 non-test production `SetStartupSubscriptions*` call sites (zero runtime `SubscribeEvents()` calls in production plugins); `redistribute`'s `IsValidNamespace("bgp")` is a build-tag guard (naturally dormant on non-BGP builds) and `exabgp/main_sdk.go` varies only wire format | `Registration.Receives` carries per-message direction/tag filters (e.g. `direction=received` on `bgp-rr`/`bgp-rs` is deadlock-critical), while wire format/encoding/envelope stays on transport/ready setup | Read of all 21 production call sites on 2026-11-24 | validated |
| A-4 | No repository test depends on a plugin being refused a send toward one peer while allowed toward another, apart from the tests that prove it | The per-peer send census (2026-11-23) | A scenario's routing depends on the per-peer refusal | The census | invalidated: the dependent tests are listed under "Per-peer send census"; each is restated with a `to` or `from` list through the judge |
| A-5 | Message names for events and for sends do not collide in one registry (`bgp:update` the send, `bgp:update-received` the event) | Checked 2026-11-24 in `internal/core/bgp/events/events.go`: both `update` and `refresh` exist in both `ValidEvents` and `BaseSendTypes` (`SendUpdate`/`SendRefresh`); under M-16 (tag filters), M-17 (direction-grouped `receive { }` / `send { }`) and Q-E4 (S17), `bgp:update` and `bgp:refresh` are single bidirectional message kinds in namespace `"bgp"` carrying tag `direction [ received \| sent ]` (matching `deliveryKey{ns, et, dir}` with zero duplication), plus `bgp:raw` and `bgp:enhanced-refresh` added to `"bgp"` | Register each BGP message name once in `"bgp"` with closed tag `direction [ received \| sent ]` where applicable; test zero duplicate `(namespace, name)` across all registered messages in Phase 2 | Read of `events.go` and `bgp/events/events.go` on 2026-11-24; reconciled with M-16, M-17 and Q-E4 | validated (unified under M-16/M-17/Q-E4 direction tag) |
| A-6 | The schema loader can apply an augment whose targets come from registrations | Verified 2026-11-24 in `config/yang/loader.go:387-416`, `loader_stages.go:29-38`, `join.go:309-352`, `publish.go:64-115`: `goyang` resolves augments during `Modules.Process()` | Add a participant-target registry in `config/yang` and synthesize one deterministic virtual module (`ze-bus-participants.yang`) emitting `augment "<target>" { container bus { uses bus:participant-bus; } }` for loaded target modules right before `l.Resolve()` in `resolveDefault`, `StageLoad`, and `resolveJoined` | Read of `config/yang` loader on 2026-11-24; Phase 4 schema tests | validated (via synthetic virtual module) |

### Risks
| ID | Risk | Early signal | Mitigation / fallback |
|----|------|--------------|----------------------|
| R-1 | A plugin starts receiving or sending on peers the operator kept it away from, because the per-peer block is gone | A converted scenario's peer receives routes it did not before | The ruled behavior change (M-4, M-12). The two censuses name every config and test it reaches; each test of the difference is restated with a list through the judge |
| R-2 | The converter corrupts `$PORT` placeholders, re-encodes test secrets, or mangles `refused=` blocks | Diff review or `TestRepositoryConfigsValidate` failures | Sentinels around parse and serialize, no `EncodeSecrets` in the converter, each `refused=` block triaged by hand |
| R-3 | `hub` validation is skipped or the `bus-hub` listener edge is dropped | Managed `.ci` or `TestConfigGraph` fails | Update the roots, the validate guard, `listenerSectionName` and the diagnostic strings together |
| R-4 | A foreign program named like a compiled-in plugin is taken for it | A unit test of a foreign `ddos-flowspec` reading the compiled plugin's state key | Identity comes from the `internal` leaf alone |
| R-5 | A forked compiled-in plugin declares something other than its registry row | The Stage 1 check table reports a difference | Stage 1 compares the declaration of an `internal ... process separate` endpoint with the registry row and refuses a difference |
| R-6 | The scale of the conversion collides with concurrent sessions in the shared checkout | Dirty files or compile conflicts | Land the engine, YANG and converter first, then convert in one focused pass, committing compiling chunks |
| R-7 | An offline `ze config validate` accepts what the daemon refuses | `test/plugin/config-validate-agrees-with-boot.ci` | Offline validation takes the program's declaration in query mode and fails when it cannot |
| R-8 | `ze config validate` now executes the programs a config names | A validate run on an untrusted file starts a program | The bus configuration is the trust boundary (S-1), query mode starts nothing, and the validate output names each program it asked |
| R-9 | A compiled-in plugin loses a command it uses because its declaration is incomplete | A functional suite fails with the "did not declare" refusal | The fixture command census and a runtime capture write each declaration once; `TestCompiledInDeclarationsCoverWhatTheySend` |
| R-10 | A filter leaks onto the delivery path | A benchmark reports an allocation, or a profile shows path text compared per message | Lists are parsed at commit and resolved when the index is built |
| R-11 | The view and the runtime differ | `TestImpliedViewMatchesCompiledFilters` | Both read the one compiled filter; no second computation exists |
| R-12 | The implied text does not round-trip | `TestImpliedTextRoundTrips` | The comment marker is `# `, never `##` at column 0 |
| R-13 | An ExaBGP script loses or gains a neighbor in the move from `feed` to `receive > from` and `send > to` | A case of `./le test functional exabgp` changes its output | The migration writes both lists from the `api` relation; the per-neighbor cases keep their expectations |
| R-14 | Restricting the route server to its client set breaks tests that run it over plain peers | Route-server `.ci` files fail with no relayed UPDATE | Census of A-10 taken 2026-11-24: 101 of 158 `.ci` files in `test/plugin/` configuring `bgp-rs` omit `rs-client`; true RS tests gain `session { rs-client true; }` and non-RS eBGP transit tests move to `bgp-rib`, routed through the test judge (Q-5, option (a)) |
| R-15 | The implied-items notice breaks a machine output (carried from the absorbed `spec-bus-2`) | A `\| json` or `ze config show` test fails | The notice is written to human channels only; a negative test asserts its absence |
| R-16 | An offline view shows a partial set as complete (carried from `spec-bus-2`) | A config with an external endpoint shows no implied reception offline | The offline view asks the program in query mode and refuses when it cannot (AC-24) |
| R-17 | A forked compiled-in plugin differs from the same plugin in the daemon (carried from the absorbed `spec-bus-3`) | A test under `./le mock plugin-external` fails where the internal run passes | Identity is the registration's on either transport, so the declaration and the profile are the same (AC-29, S28) |
| R-18 | The security test is green for a reason other than the check (carried from `spec-bus-3`) | The negative passes before the fix is written | The negative is recorded red first, against today's code, and its red output is kept (AC-26, S20) |
| R-19 | A reload that removes a send allow-list entry leaves the old profile in force (carried from `spec-bus-3`) | A command still succeeds after the reload | The profile lives in the policy generation the reload swaps (AC-27, S27) |

## Blast Radius

| Question | Answer |
|----------|--------|
| What breaks if this is wrong? | Configs fail to parse, plugins receive nothing or everything, a plugin sends what it must not or is refused what it needs, managed hub listeners fail to bind, aliased plugins fail state access, or a plugin command is authorized wrongly |
| How is it reverted? | Git revert of the cutover commits; pre-release `main` has no deployed user |
| Who else touches this path? | Any concurrent spec that adds `.ci` tests with `plugin {`, `attach process` or `exabgp { bridge`, and any that adds an engine operation |

## Wiring Test (MANDATORY -- NOT deferrable)

| Entry Point | -> | Feature Code | Test |
|-------------|---|--------------|------|
| `ze start` of `bus { endpoint collector { external { run ...; } } }` with pure `bgp { }` | -> | Registration -> compiled filter -> delivery index -> BGP event delivery | `test/plugin/bus-default-wiring.ci` (new) |
| `ze start` with a `bus { send { to [ ... ]; } }` block on a peer and on a group | -> | Inheritance -> compilation -> index | `test/plugin/bus-to-replaces-default.ci`, `test/plugin/bus-group-inheritance.ci` (new) |
| `ze start` with `receive > from`, per-message lists and both allow-lists | -> | Compilation -> index and engine-operation check | `test/plugin/bus-from-limits.ci`, `test/plugin/bus-allow-lists.ci` (new) |
| `ze config validate` and an editor commit of each refused config | -> | The eleven commit refusals | `test/parse/bus-commit-refusals.ci`, `test/editor/bus-block-commit.et` (new) |
| `ze config validate` and commit of a redistribution rule whose Loc-RIB or orchestrator a `bus` block cuts, and the other feature requirements | -> | The requirement check against the compiled filters | `test/parse/bus-requirement-refusals.ci` (new; S18, S33 to S35) |
| `show event delivery` on a daemon with edges of every origin | -> | The compiled filters -> `handleShowEventDelivery` | `test/plugin/bus-delivery-origins.ci` (new; AC-25) |
| A program that calls `route-install` without declaring `system-rib:route-install` | -> | The message field on its `engineOps` entry -> the shared dispatch check | `test/plugin/bus-send-operation-capability.ci` (new; S37) |
| Two participants announce one prefix; one goes away; then the keep option | -> | `originReceiptStore.endOwner` -> withdrawal per participant | `test/plugin/bus-route-ownership.ci` (new; S38) |
| A `receive { message <name> { tag <t> [ ... ]; } }` line on a receiver | -> | Compilation of condition 7 -> index | `test/plugin/bus-tag-filter.ci` (new; S41) |
| `bus { participant /bgp/group/transit { ... } }` beside the group's own block | -> | Two places -> one filter | `test/plugin/bus-central-participant.ci` (new; S39) |
| Two ExaBGP script endpoints that both declare route refresh | -> | Stage 3 `declare-capabilities` -> `CapabilityInjector.planLocked` per participant | `test/plugin/exabgp-virtual-session-capabilities.ci` (new; S40) |
| An ExaBGP script endpoint whose script exits | -> | The wrapper exits with its script -> the process manager's restart (`external { respawn }`) -> `endOwner` withdrawal -> Stage 3 `declare-capabilities` of the new incarnation | `test/plugin/exabgp-script-exit.ci` (new; S42) |
| `bgp { route-server { bus { ... } } }` and `bus { endpoint bgp-rs { ... } }` together | -> | Two places -> one filter -> `\| implied` | `test/plugin/bus-two-places.ci` (new) |
| `ze --plugin "<program>" -` | -> | `MergeCliPlugins` -> first pass -> held Stage 1 declaration | `test/plugin/external-yang-command-line.ci` (existing, converted, not weakened) |
| `bus { endpoint my-ddos { internal ddos-flowspec; process separate; } }`, then `show doctor` | -> | Process identity -> `dispatch_state.go` and `doctor_snapshot.go` | `test/plugin/bus-aliased-forked-state-doctor.ci` (new) |
| `ze config validate <file>` with no daemon, on a config with an external endpoint | -> | `config.SchemaWithDeclaredPlugins` -> `plugin.QueryDeclaration` -> module join -> `bus` validation | `test/parse/bus-offline-validate-query-mode.ci` (new) |
| `ze start` with `bgp-rs` and `bgp-rr` both loaded, client and internal peers, nothing written | -> | Each plugin's own session-type limit -> one replay per peer | `test/plugin/bus-rs-rr-one-box.ci` (new) |
| An external program dispatching a command it did not declare | -> | Shared dispatch path -> filter -> `authz` profile -> refusal, counter, doctor event | `test/plugin/bus-send-undeclared-refused.ci` (new) |
| `show config running \| implied` on the CLI, the editor and the web | -> | The registered pipe operation -> the compiled filters | `test/plugin/bus-implied-view.ci`, `test/editor/bus-implied-editor.et`, `test/web/bus-implied-workbench.wb` (new; the web suite's files are `.wb`) |
| `show config running \| implied` on a peer with a `role` and no export next-hop member written, and on a peer whose export list names `next-hop:unchanged` | -> | The one implied-filter seam (built-in `loop-detection` and trailing `next-hop:auto`, the plugin's `FilterInsertions`, the any-peer `bgp-role:otc-egress` on every peer) -> the filter members of the view, in chain position | `test/plugin/bus-implied-view.ci` (new; filter-member case, S43) |
| `ze exabgp migrate` of a config with two processes and per-neighbor `api` blocks, then `ze start` | -> | Migration writes endpoints with `receive > from` and `send > to` -> the wrapper -> delivery and command scope | `test/plugin/exabgp-script-endpoints.ci` (new; S17) |
| SIGHUP that writes a send allow-list omitting the command path an ExaBGP script's route line becomes | -> | Reload -> the endpoint's recompiled filter -> the shared dispatch check refuses the translated command -> the wrapper answers the script `error` in its API version's form (`AckMode.WriteError`) | `test/plugin/exabgp-script-send-narrowed.ci` (new; S44) |
| SIGHUP after changing a list on a running participant | -> | Reload -> recompilation -> index rebuild | `test/reload/bus-list-reload.ci` (new) |

## Acceptance Criteria

| AC ID | Input / Condition | Expected Behavior | Permitted Survivors and Why |
|-------|-------------------|-------------------|-----------------------------|
| AC-1 | An endpoint whose program registers the reception of `bgp:update-received` and `bgp:state` and declares no send; pure `bgp { }`; nothing else written | The config validates and boots. The program receives both messages for every peer, including a peer added by a later reload. A send of `bgp:update` is refused because it was not declared | A message the program registered no reception for is never delivered |
| AC-2 | `send { to [ ... ]; }` written on a peer, on a group, and present and empty | The written list is the complete destination set of that participant's messages: the listed participants receive them and registered receivers outside it receive nothing. A group's list applies to its peers; a member's list replaces it. The empty list delivers to nobody | Participants with no list keep the registered wiring |
| AC-3 | `receive { from [ ... ]; }` absent, written, and present and empty, on a receiver | Absent: the receiver gets the message from every sender. Written: from the senders the list covers only. Empty: from nobody | The receiver's own sends are unaffected |
| AC-4 | A block-level `send > to` and a `send { message <name> { to [ ... ]; } }` on one participant; the same for `receive > from` and `receive { message <name> { from [ ... ]; } }` | The named message follows its own list; every other message follows the block-level list | None: one list decides each message |
| AC-5 | A send allow-list and a receive allow-list, each absent, partial and empty | Absent: everything declared passes. Partial: the listed names pass and the others are refused. Empty: nothing passes. Each refusal names the participant, the message and the line that admits it, and counts once | `show config` keeps an empty list distinct from an absent one |
| AC-6 | Each of the eleven commit refusals of "Two places to write, one filter" | Refused at `ze config validate`, editor commit, reload and `ze start` with one verdict, naming the line, the value and what is valid; refusals 5 and 6 name both lines | The corrected config of each case loads |
| AC-7 | A plugin with an endpoint entry and a config section of its own, with a block in one place, in the other, in both agreeing, and in both disagreeing | The first three load and yield the same single filter, which the display shows once. The fourth is refused naming both lines | Identical content in both places is permitted because it states one filter |
| AC-8 | A registered reception whose sender is absent, then configured on restart (external endpoint, per `RefuseChangedDeclarations`) or by reload (compiled-in sender) | The config loads, and `show doctor`, the reconcile and the display name the reception as dormant. Once the sender is configured and running, the delivery starts | A written list naming an unknown path is still refused (AC-6); adding an external endpoint on reload is still refused by `RefuseChangedDeclarations` naming `restart ze` |
| AC-9 | `hub { server ...; client ...; }` under `bus { hub { ... } }` | The managed hub listener binds and validates, `show listeners` reports `bus-hub`, `config graph` links `bus` to `bus-hub`, and the managed doctor checks pass | None: the block only moves |
| AC-10 | A config that uses the retired `plugin { }`, `attach process`, `process` under `bgp`, or `exabgp { bridge }` | Refused at parse with `RetiredKeywordHint` pointing to `bus` | None: no hybrid is accepted |
| AC-11 | `ze --plugin ze.bgp-bmp -` and `ze --plugin "<program>" -` | Both start the plugin they name. The program's module is joined before the full parse and its declarations are honored as a config endpoint's are | Offline `ze bgp decode --plugin` and `ze plugin test --plugin` keep accepting external decoders |
| AC-12 | An ExaBGP config with two processes, `p1` with `encoder json` and `p2` with `respawn false` and `on-exit withdraw`; per-neighbor `api` blocks that grant `p1` different message kinds from two neighbors, one of them with a `packets` word; a third neighbor naming `p2` and no message kind; through `ze exabgp migrate`, then `ze start` on the output | The output loads with no edit. It holds no `exabgp` root, no `attach process` and no internal `exabgp-bridge`. It holds one endpoint per process whose `run` is `ze exabgp plugin` with the options the blocks stated (`--encoder json` and one `--form <neighbor>:receive-packets` on `p1`) and none for a default; `p2`'s endpoint holds `external { respawn false; }` and no keep option, `on-exit withdraw` being the endpoint's default, and no `run` line holds `--respawn` or `--on-exit` (E-6; a process with `on-exit keep`, or held to API version 4 with no `on-exit`, gets the keep option, AC-41), and the `receive > from`, `send > to`, `receive > allow` and `receive > message <kind> > from` lines "ExaBGP" derives. Each script is written only the events its neighbors granted, in the form each asked. A command with no neighbor selector reaches the endpoint's `to` only, and no refusal is logged. Every case of `./le test functional exabgp` keeps its expectations | Negative: a config that still holds `exabgp { bridge }` is refused at parse (AC-10) |
| AC-13 | `bus { endpoint my-ddos { internal ddos-flowspec; } }`, the same with `process separate;`, and `bus { endpoint ddos-flowspec { external { run ...; } } }` | Both compiled endpoints read and write the plugin's state key, report healthy in `show doctor`, hold the same registered wiring, and block `ze data rm` while running. The foreign program holds none of the compiled plugin's grants. The five sibling sites (`RequiresPeerUpBarrier`, `configRefusalIsFatal`, `isPluginLoaded`, `collectOrphanedDependencies`, `appendHeldNeedProviders`) resolve by registration name | `process separate` on a plugin whose registration sets `RunsInProcessOnly` is refused at commit with that reason |
| AC-14 | A redistribution rule importing from and exporting to BGP, a FlowSpec family, `aigp` written on a peer, and a filter member owned by a plugin: first with nothing written, then with an override that removes what the feature needs | With nothing written all four work. With the override, validate, commit, reload and start refuse with one message naming the feature, the participant, the removing line and the completing line as a typed fix; completing it loads | An unrelated participant with an empty allow-list is not refused |
| AC-15 | iBGP peers with no `aigp` written and the Loc-RIB's reception cut by an override | The config loads. On the default configuration with nothing written, a route carrying AIGP is compared by AIGP | Only written `aigp` activates the requirement |
| AC-16 | Every tracked `.conf`, `.ci`, `.et`, interop scenario, fixture, doc and website example | `TestRepositoryConfigsValidate` and the scoped functional suites pass, and the converted interop scenarios pass against their peers. None keeps `plugin {`, `attach process` or `exabgp { bridge` outside `plan/` history, and the converter wrote no `bus` block by default | History under `plan/journal/`, `plan/learned/`, `plan/handover/` and `plan/records/` may quote the old syntax |
| AC-17 | A broadcast, an addressed message and the send check, under a benchmark, with `to` and `from` lists naming a peer, a group and `/bgp` | Each delivers to the receivers the lists select, at zero allocations per operation. No path text is parsed or compared per message | None: the lists are resolved at build |
| AC-18 | `ze config validate` with no daemon, on a config with an external endpoint: with a program that answers in query mode, with a list naming a message that program did not declare, and with a `run` line that names no program | The first validates. The second is refused with what the program declared. The third fails naming the endpoint, the `run` line, the cause and `ze show plugin conformance run "<run line>"`. The program's run loop never starts | A running daemon is still asked first |
| AC-19 | A route server with three connected clients; a reflector with a client and a non-client; both plugins loaded on one box with client and internal peers; nothing written | The route server forwards a client's routes to every other client. The reflector applies RFC 4456 Section 6. With both loaded, each peer receives each route once and one replay at peer-up | An export policy on one peer still keeps a route from it |
| AC-20 | A commit that deletes a group, a peer or an endpoint that a `to` or `from` names, in the editor, by reload and by validate of the candidate | Refused, naming the line and the deleted entry. Changing the line in the same commit makes it pass | Deleting an entry no list names is accepted |
| AC-21 | A dynamic group, a receiver whose `receive > from` names that group's path, and a peer that connects from the group's range after start; then a receiver whose `receive > from` names `/bgp/ip/<address>` inside the range (M-3, M-18) | The dynamic peer's `bgp:state` and updates are delivered to that receiver with no config change. A dynamic peer of another group delivers nothing to it. The line naming `/bgp/ip/<address>` commits, is shown dormant while no peer has that address, and once a peer connects from it that peer's messages are delivered to the receiver and those of another peer of the same range are not | A line naming `/bgp/ip/<address>` outside every dynamic group's range is refused at commit, the message listing the ranges (M-18) |
| AC-22 | A list naming a peer by name, the same peer by address, a group, and `/bgp` | Name and address give the same filter. The group covers its peers; `/bgp` covers every peer. `show config` prints the path as written | None: both spellings are accepted |
| AC-23 | Implied endpoints, registered wiring, a written `to`, an inherited list, a required delivery and a dormant reception, viewed with `\| implied` on the CLI (three config commands), the editor, the web inspection, the workbench, REST, gRPC and MCP | Every item is shown with its origin and matches what runs. Beside a written `to` the replaced registered destinations are shown. A human display carries the notice; machine outputs do not. With `\| json` the envelope holds `config` and `implied`; without `\| implied` the output is byte-identical to today's | A written endpoint appears once, as written. gNMI `Get` stays written-only |
| AC-24 | `show config source <file> \| implied` with no daemon, for a file with an external endpoint: with a program that answers in query mode, and with a `run` line that names no program | The first shows the program's registered wiring as implied, and its run loop never starts. The second is refused, naming the endpoint, the `run` line, the cause and the conformance command | None: a partial set is never shown |
| AC-25 | A running daemon with edges of every origin | `show event delivery` lists each edge once with its origin and both participant paths, across every namespace, and `ze config graph` draws the same set. A dynamic peer's edge shows the path of the group that admitted it beside its address (S31) | None: every edge carries an origin |
| AC-26 | A program Ze did not ship dispatches `clear bgp peer`, which it did not declare; nothing written | Refused with the "did not declare" message. The counter increments once and doctor reports `CodeDoctorPluginRequestRefused` for that participant. Against today's code this test is red: the command runs | Operators on every surface are unaffected |
| AC-27 | The program declares `cli:show bgp peer` and `cli:clear bgp peer`; the operator writes a send allow-list with the first, then an empty one | With the first list `show` passes and `clear` is refused with the allow-list message and its fix line. With the empty list both are refused. With none both pass, and `show interface` is refused as undeclared | Only declared and admitted paths pass |
| AC-28 | A program calls `route-install`, `route-remove` or `inject-wire-route` without declaring the message, then with it | Refused without; succeeds with, each of the three (S37) | None: every such operation is gated |
| AC-29 | A compiled-in plugin, in the daemon and under `./le mock plugin-external`, runs its normal work; then it is given an allow-list that removes a command it uses | Every command succeeds in both transports with nothing written. With the list the command is refused in the same message shape as AC-27 | None: a compiled-in plugin refused a command it uses with nothing written is a defect of its declaration |
| AC-30 | A plugin calls `emit-event` for a message it did not register as a send | Refused. With the send registered, the message is delivered to its registered receivers | None: an emitter owns what it registered |
| AC-31 | A reload adds, changes and removes a list or an allow-list entry while the participant runs | The next message follows the new filter, and the participant and every BGP session keep running across the reload | None: a filter change restarts nothing |
| AC-32 | Every entry of `engineOps` and every operation registered through `RegisterEngineOp` | The walk test passes over both registries and reports, for each operation, the message it carries or its written reason. A test entry with neither makes the walk fail, naming that entry | An entry with `none` and a reason is permitted because the reason is reviewed with the entry |
| AC-33 | An ExaBGP script whose endpoint has nothing written sends a route line; then a reload writes a send allow-list on that endpoint that omits `"cli:send bgp"` (the command path the translator dispatches via `dispatch-command`), and the script sends the same line | Before the reload the line is answered `done`. After it the line is answered `error` in the form of the script's API version (`{"error": "<why>"}` for API 6, `error: <why>` for API 4 or undecided, then the marker, `AckMode.WriteError`), no UPDATE is sent, and the refusal is counted once under the endpoint's name. Peer selection is judged against `send > to` while `"cli:send bgp"` is checked by `send > allow` (Q-T5, option (a)) | Negative: a line naming a neighbor outside the endpoint's `to` is answered `error` (`servedSelector`, `errSelectorMatchesNoPeer`, fed by Q-E3 option (a)); an explicit `neighbor *` / `peer *` narrows to `to` (`t.everyPeer()`); a line naming no neighbor is answered `done` and counts no refusal while its command is allowed, as before the reload |
| AC-34 | The plugin-less in-process RPC identity | Its commands are authorized as today, and every caller of it is listed with its reason | Permitted because it names no participant, so no filter exists to judge it |
| AC-35 | A plugin process sends a message naming another participant as sender | The bus stamps the sender from the authenticated registration, and the message is filtered and delivered as the real sender's | A process that registered participants (BGP for its peers) names them |
| AC-36 | A dynamic group whose members need an implied endpoint | The endpoint is loaded and shown by `\| implied` | None: a dynamic group implies what a static one does |
| AC-37 | A route server with no `bus` block written and three connected clients, viewed with `\| implied` and `show event delivery` | The route server's `bgp:update` send is shown once, as registered, under its own path, not once per peer; every connected client is listed as a participant its registered destinations cover | None: one registration is one line |
| AC-38 | An implied item printed by `show config running \| implied`, pasted back as written configuration and committed | The commit succeeds, and the item is then shown once, as written, with no implied copy beside it | None: the printed form is the stored short path form |
| AC-39 | A process that stops being fed by a peer, and an OPEN validator declaring `WantsValidateOpen`, when a peer is removed | Both deliveries are registered receptions: each is delivered through the compiled filter, shown on `show event delivery` with origin `registered`, and removed by a `receive` allow-list that excludes it | None: no delivery bypasses the filter |
| AC-40 | A participant that declared `bgp:update` and two `cli:` paths, with `send { allow [ bgp:update ]; }` | `bgp:update` passes; each declared `cli:` path is refused with the allow-list message and its fix line | None: one send allow-list speaks for every namespace |
| AC-41 | Participants A and B both announce route R toward one peer; then A goes away; then A alone announces R and goes away; then the same with `external { on-exit keep; }` on A's endpoint; then `ze exabgp migrate` of four ExaBGP processes: one with `on-exit keep`, one with `on-exit withdraw`, one held to API version 4 with no `on-exit`, one with `respawn false` | After A goes while B holds R, the peer still has R. After A alone goes, R is withdrawn from the peer (and any watchdog routes it gave `bgp-watchdog` are forgotten via the participant-exit lifecycle message, Q-E11). With `external { on-exit keep; }`, R stays after A goes. The migration writes `external { on-exit keep; }` on the endpoints of the first and third processes and not on the second, and `external { respawn false; }` on the fourth's; no `run` line holds `--on-exit` or `--respawn` (E-2, E-6, Q-E11) | None: a participant's routes follow its presence, as a peer's do |
| AC-42 | A group limited in its own section, centrally by `bus { participant /bgp/group/transit { ... } }`, in both identically, and in both differently | The first three load and compile to the same filter, shown once with both lines as origin where both exist. The fourth is refused naming both lines. An endpoint's own `send`/`receive` fields stay beside `participant /bus/endpoint/<name>` judged by refusal 6, and a group-level `participant` applies at the group's rank so a member's own line replaces it item by item (Q-10, options (a) and (i)) | None |
| AC-43 | Two ExaBGP script endpoints whose `run` lines both hold `--route-refresh`, so both translators declare route refresh at Stage 3; two endpoints declaring different ADD-PATH modes toward one peer; and `ze exabgp migrate` of an ExaBGP config whose neighbor names both processes in its `api` block and states route refresh and two families, then the same with route refresh removed from that neighbor | Both endpoints declaring identical code and bytes start, the startup tier does not fail on a capability conflict, and the peer's OPEN carries route refresh once. Two endpoints declaring different values of one capability toward one real peer are refused at start naming both endpoints, the capability, the two values and the line to change (Q-E9, option (a)). The migration writes on each endpoint's `run` line one `--family` per family the neighbor states (union across neighbors naming the process) and `--route-refresh`, taken from the ExaBGP configuration (E-7), and refuses conflicting ADD-PATH modes across neighbors naming one process; with route refresh removed, no `run` line holds `--route-refresh` and no translator declares code 2 | None |
| AC-44 | A receiver with `receive { message bgp:route-change { tag source [ bgp ]; } }`; a `tag` line naming a tag the message does not carry; a value outside the tag's closed set | A message with `source bgp` is delivered and one with another value is not. The undeclared tag is refused at commit listing the declared tags (refusal 9). The value outside the set is refused listing the set (refusal 10) | A receiver with no tag filter receives every value and pays no comparison |
| AC-45 | An ExaBGP script endpoint whose script announces a route toward one peer and then exits while the daemon runs: with nothing written in its `external` block, with `respawn false`, and with `external { on-exit keep; }`; then `ze exabgp plugin <script>` run with no hub token, whose script exits | The wrapper process exits when its script exits, with the script's exit status, and restarts nothing itself (E-6). The route is withdrawn from the peer (E-2). With nothing written, Ze's process manager starts the endpoint again, wrapper and script together, as a new process; the new incarnation's Stage 3 declaration of its virtual session capabilities is accepted (E-4) and the script's route reaches the peer again. With `respawn false` the endpoint stays stopped and the route stays withdrawn. With `external { on-exit keep; }` the route stays after the exit. The standalone runner writes no withdrawals to stdout and exits with its script's exit status (E-6). The compatibility cases `api-on-exit`, `api-on-exit-configured`, `api-on-exit-v4-keep` and `api-no-respawn` of `./le test functional exabgp`, run from migrated output, keep their expectations (AC-12) | Negative: against today's code the wrapper forks the script again itself (`script.supervise`, `Respawn: true` in `runSDKMode`) while the wrapper's own process keeps running, so the test is recorded red first. A restart past the process manager's limit leaves the endpoint disabled (`ErrProcessDisabled`), with the route withdrawn |
| AC-46 | A peer with a `role` written and no export next-hop member written; a peer whose export filter list names `next-hop:unchanged`; a peer whose export filter list names `next-hop:auto`; each viewed with `\| implied` on every surface AC-23 names | Every filter member Ze inserts is shown beside the written filter list, in its chain position, with its direction, reference and `implied-by` reason, read from the one implied-filter seam (core-design decision "Every automatic filter-chain member Ze inserts", 2026-11-23): the built-in `loop-detection` at the head of every import list and the trailing `next-hop:auto` at the tail of an export list that holds no unconditional next-hop member, each naming the built-in rule that inserts it, and each member a plugin's `FilterInsertions` places (the role's `bgp-role:otc-ingress` and `bgp-role:otc-egress`), naming the config path that inserted it. A member declared with `Scope: rpc.FilterScopeAnyPeer`, as `bgp-role:otc-egress` is (`otcInsertions`, `role/otc_filter.go`), is placed at the head of every peer's export list once one peer meets its condition (`anyPeerHolds`, `insertionHolds`, `insertForPeer` in `bgp/config/filter_insertion.go`), so it is shown on every peer it lands on, each time with the config path of the peer whose config placed it. The members shown are the chain that runs. With `\| json` they are under `implied.filters` | A member the operator wrote appears once, as written: the written `next-hop:auto` has no implied copy, and the written `next-hop:unchanged` leaves no implied `next-hop:auto` (core-design decision "Built-in `next-hop:auto` ...", which appends it only when no unconditional `next-hop:self`, `next-hop:unchanged` or `next-hop:auto` is present). No member name is written in the view's code |

## Worked scenarios

Forty-four scenarios. Each becomes the named test; the implementer writes the test from the row. Common setup unless a row says otherwise: peers `edge1` (192.0.2.1) and `lab` (192.0.2.9) standalone, group `transit` with peers `t1` and `t2`; endpoint `collector`, a fixture program that registers the reception of `bgp:state` and `bgp:update-received` and writes each message it receives to a file the test reads; the Loc-RIB loaded with nothing written, named here `/bus/endpoint/bgp-rib`. Every `bus` block is written in the direction-grouped shape (M-17), and an empty list is written with the keyword `none` (`send { to none; }`, `receive { from none; }`, `send { allow none; }`, `receive { allow none; }`, Q-1 option (a)). A refusal's required content is listed; its literal text follows `ai/rules/user-facing-errors.md` and is fixed in the test when written.

| # | Configuration | Stimulus | Expected observation | Negative that discriminates | AC and test |
|---|---------------|----------|----------------------|-----------------------------|-------------|
| S1 | `bus { endpoint collector { external { run ./collector; } } }` and pure `bgp { }` | `edge1` establishes and sends one UPDATE; then a reload adds peer `new1`, which establishes | `collector` records `bgp:state` and `bgp:update-received` for `edge1`, then `bgp:state` for `new1`. `show config running \| implied` shows the collector's two receptions as registered | `collector` sends `bgp:update` (undeclared): refused, message names `collector` and `bgp:update`, counter 1 | AC-1; `test/plugin/bus-default-wiring.ci` |
| S2 | As S1, plus `bgp { peer lab { bus { send { to [ /bus/endpoint/collector ]; } } } }` | `lab` and `edge1` each send one UPDATE | `collector` records both. The Loc-RIB holds `edge1`'s route and not `lab`'s. `\| implied` shows `lab`'s `send > to` as written and lists the Loc-RIB among the registered destinations it replaced | Without the line, the Loc-RIB holds `lab`'s route | AC-2, AC-23; `test/plugin/bus-to-replaces-default.ci` |
| S3 | As S1, plus `bgp { peer lab { bus { send { to none; } } } }` | `lab` establishes and sends one UPDATE | No participant records anything for `lab`: `collector` holds no `lab` message, the Loc-RIB no `lab` route. `show config` prints `to none` | `edge1`'s messages still arrive; combining `to [ none /bus/endpoint/collector ]` is refused at commit | AC-2; `test/plugin/bus-to-replaces-default.ci` (second case) |
| S4 | Three configs on `collector`: no `receive > from`; `receive { from [ /bgp/peer/edge1 ]; }`; `receive { from none; }` | `edge1` and `lab` each establish | First: both recorded. Second: `edge1` only. Third: none | In the second, the Loc-RIB still holds `lab`'s route: `receive > from` on one receiver removes nothing from another | AC-3; `test/plugin/bus-from-limits.ci` |
| S5 | `bgp { peer lab { bus { send { to [ /bus/endpoint/bgp-rib ]; message bgp:state { to [ /bus/endpoint/collector ]; } } } } }` | `lab` establishes and sends one UPDATE | `collector` records `bgp:state` for `lab` and no `bgp:update-received`. The Loc-RIB holds `lab`'s route | Removing the `send > message bgp:state` entry: `collector` records nothing for `lab` | AC-4; `test/plugin/bus-from-limits.ci` (per-message case) |
| S6 | `bgp { group transit { bus { send { to [ /bus/endpoint/bgp-rib ]; } receive { from [ /bus/endpoint/bgp-rib ]; } } peer t1 { bus { send { to [ /bus/endpoint/collector ]; } } } } }`, and a fixture endpoint `sender` that declares `bgp:update` | `t1` and `t2` each send one UPDATE; `sender` sends an update addressed to `t1` | `t2`: Loc-RIB only. `t1`: `collector` only, because the member's `send > to` replaces the group's line whole (M-14). `t1` keeps the group's `receive > from`, which it did not write, and `\| implied` shows it on `t1` as inherited from `transit` | `sender`'s update addressed to `t1` is not delivered, because the inherited `receive > from` covers the Loc-RIB only; with `t1 { bus { receive { from [ /bus/endpoint/sender ]; } } }` added, it is delivered, and the group's line no longer applies to `t1` | AC-2, AC-23; `test/plugin/bus-group-inheritance.ci`, `TestBusBlockInheritance` |
| S7 | A fixture endpoint `sender` that declares `bgp:update` and `bgp:refresh`: no `send`; `send { allow [ bgp:update ]; }`; `send { allow none; }`; `send { allow [ bgp:raw ]; }` | `sender` sends an update and a refresh to `edge1` | None: both reach `edge1`. Partial: the update arrives, the refresh is refused naming `sender`, `bgp:refresh` and the line `set bus endpoint sender send allow bgp:refresh`. Empty (`allow none`): both refused. The fourth config is refused at commit, listing `bgp:update` and `bgp:refresh` as what `sender` declared | The counter is 0, 1, 2 across the first three | AC-5, AC-6; `test/plugin/bus-allow-lists.ci`, `test/parse/bus-commit-refusals.ci` |
| S8 | On `collector`: no `receive`; `receive { allow [ bgp:state ]; }`; `receive { allow none; }` | `edge1` establishes and sends one UPDATE | None: both messages recorded. Partial: `bgp:state` only. Empty (`allow none`): nothing | The Loc-RIB still holds the route in all three | AC-5; `test/plugin/bus-allow-lists.ci` (receive case) |
| S9 | On `sender`: `send { allow [ bgp:update ]; to [ /bgp/group/transit ]; }`; then the same with `message bgp:refresh { to [ /bgp/peer/edge1 ]; }` added inside `send` | `sender` sends an update with no selector | First: `t1` and `t2` receive it, `edge1` and `lab` do not. Second: refused at commit, naming the `send allow` line and the `send message bgp:refresh` line | In the first, a refresh is refused by the allow-list, not by `send > to` | AC-5, AC-6; `test/plugin/bus-allow-lists.ci`, `test/parse/bus-commit-refusals.ci` |

| S43 | `edge1` with `role { import customer; }` and no `filter` written; `lab` with `filter { export { filters [ next-hop:unchanged ]; final accept; } }`; `t1` with `filter { export { filters [ next-hop:auto ]; final accept; } }` | `show config running \| implied`; `show config running \| implied \| json`; the same on the editor and the web inspection | `edge1`'s import list shows `loop-detection` first, implied by the built-in loop-detection step, then `bgp-role:otc-ingress`, implied by `edge1`'s `role import` line and marked required with its reason; its export list shows `bgp-role:otc-egress` first, implied by the same line and marked required with its reason, and `next-hop:auto` last, implied by the trailing next-hop rule. `bgp-role:otc-egress` is an any-peer insertion (`otcInsertions` declares `Scope: rpc.FilterScopeAnyPeer`; `insertionHolds` holds it on every peer once `anyPeerHolds` finds `edge1` meeting it, and `insertForPeer` prepends it), so `lab`, `t1` and `t2` each show it too, at the head of their export list, implied by `edge1`'s `role import` line (another peer's path) and marked required. Every import list shows the implied `loop-detection` first. `lab`'s export list then shows the written `next-hop:unchanged` once and no implied `next-hop:auto`. `t1`'s shows the written `next-hop:auto` once, as written, with no implied copy. `t2`'s shows the implied `next-hop:auto` last. Under `implied.filters` each member carries its direction, position, reference and `implied-by`, and the list for each peer equals the chain the reactor compiled for it | With `role` removed from `edge1`, the `bgp-role:` members leave every peer's view while `loop-detection` and each implied `next-hop:auto` stay. A view that hid the built-in steps, or that named members itself rather than reading the seam, fails the comparison with the compiled chain | AC-46, AC-23; `test/plugin/bus-implied-view.ci` (filter-member case), `TestImpliedShowsInsertedFilterMembers`, `TestImpliedViewMatchesCompiledFilters` |
| S44 | `bus { endpoint s1 { external { run "ze exabgp plugin ./s1.py"; } } }`, nothing else written on `s1`; run twice, once with `s1.py` writing `api version 6` first and once with `api version 4` | `s1.py` writes `announce route 203.0.113.0/24 next-hop self` with no neighbor. A reload then writes on `s1` a send allow-list holding every name the wrapper declares except `"cli:send bgp"` (the `cli:` prefix of the `send bgp <sel> update text ...` command `bridgerun/script.go` dispatches via `dispatch-command`), and `s1.py` writes `announce route 198.51.100.0/24 next-hop self` | Before the reload the line is answered `done` and `edge1` receives 203.0.113.0/24. After it the line is answered `{"error": "<why>"}` then `error` in the API 6 run, and `error: <why>` then `error` in the API 4 run (`AckMode.WriteError`); `edge1` receives no UPDATE for 198.51.100.0/24; the refusal counter for `/bus/endpoint/s1` reads 1, and `s1` keeps running across the reload | Before any reload, with `send { to [ /bgp/peer/edge1 ]; }` on `s1`: the line `neighbor 192.0.2.9 announce route 203.0.113.0/24 next-hop self` names `lab`, outside `to`, and is answered `error` (`Translator.servedSelector`, `errSelectorMatchesNoPeer`), and `lab` receives nothing; an explicit `peer * announce route ...` narrows to `edge1` (`t.everyPeer()`); the line naming no neighbor is answered `done` and the refusal counter stays 0. Peer selection is judged against `send > to` while `"cli:send bgp"` is checked by `send > allow` (Q-E3 option (a), Q-T5 option (a)) | AC-33; `test/plugin/exabgp-script-send-narrowed.ci` |

Coverage: AC-1 S1; AC-2 S2, S3, S6, S14; AC-3 S4; AC-4 S5; AC-5 S7, S8, S9, S11; AC-6 S7, S9, S10, S12, S41; AC-7 S10, S14; AC-8 S21; AC-9 S23; AC-10 S23; AC-11 S16; AC-12 S17; AC-13 S15; AC-14 S18, S33, S34, S35; AC-15 S26; AC-16 S24; AC-17 S24; AC-18 S16; AC-19 S13; AC-20 S19; AC-21 S22; AC-22 S12; AC-23 S2, S6, S10, S11, S29, S30, S43; AC-24 S16; AC-25 S21, S31; AC-26 S20; AC-27 S27; AC-28 S20, S37; AC-29 S28; AC-30 S20, S37; AC-31 S19; AC-32 S20; AC-33 S17, S44; AC-34 S20; AC-35 S25; AC-36 S22; AC-37 S29; AC-38 S30; AC-39 S32; AC-40 S36; AC-41 S38; AC-42 S39; AC-43 S40; AC-44 S41; AC-45 S42; AC-46 S43. Every criterion has at least one scenario.

## End-to-End User Stories

| # | User does | Path through system | Test proving it works |
|---|-----------|--------------------|-----------------------|
| 1 | Declares an external collector as an endpoint and writes nothing else | Stage 1 declaration -> registered wiring -> delivery for every peer | `test/plugin/bus-default-wiring.ci` |
| 2 | Keeps a lab peer's routes out of the RIB with one `send > to` line on the peer | Inheritance -> compiled filter -> index | `test/plugin/bus-to-replaces-default.ci` |
| 3 | Limits a third-party program from the bus side | Endpoint fields -> compiled filter -> engine-operation check | `test/plugin/bus-central-limit.ci` |
| 4 | Asks what Ze does on their behalf | `show config running \| implied` -> compiled filters | `test/plugin/bus-implied-view.ci` |
| 5 | Runs a compiled-in plugin as a separate process under a name of their own and checks `show doctor` | Endpoint `process separate` -> fork of Ze's own binary -> identity by registration | `test/plugin/bus-aliased-forked-state-doctor.ci` |
| 6 | Migrates an ExaBGP config with processes and runs it | `ze exabgp migrate` -> `ze start` -> the wrapper | `test/plugin/exabgp-script-endpoints.ci` |
| 7 | Configures the managed hub under `bus { hub }` | First pass -> `ExtractHubConfig` -> `show listeners` | `test/managed/bus-hub-listener.ci` |
| 8 | Narrows the Loc-RIB below what a redistribution rule needs, commits, reads the refusal with its typed fix, applies the fix, and the config loads (absorbed `spec-bus-2`, story 2) | Commit -> requirement check against the compiled filters -> refusal naming the feature, the participant, the removing line and the completing line -> the completing line applied -> load | `test/parse/bus-requirement-refusals.ci` (S18) |
| 9 | Runs a monitoring script that declares two `show` commands, then narrows it to one with a send allow-list by reload (absorbed `spec-bus-3`, story 1) | Stage 1 declaration -> the participant's filter -> the shared dispatch check; reload -> recompiled filter | `test/plugin/bus-send-undeclared-refused.ci`, `test/reload/bus-send-allow-list-reload.ci` (S20, S27) |
| 10 | Finds out why a plugin's command fails (absorbed `spec-bus-3`, story 2) | `show doctor` -> `CodeDoctorPluginRequestRefused` with the participant, the reason and the fix line; the refusal counter on the metrics endpoint | `test/plugin/bus-send-refusal-doctor.ci` |

### Tests routed through the test judge

Each of these proves something the rework changes or removes. The author does not judge them (`ai/META-DECISIONS.md`, "Test changes"). Under M-12 the converter writes nothing, so a test of a difference is restated with a `to` or `from` list, which makes the per-peer cases expressible again.

| Test | What it proves today | What the rework does to it |
|------|----------------------|----------------------------|
| `test/plugin/attach-process-send-permission.ci` | A process is refused a send toward a peer that does not grant it, and an unattached process is silent | Restated: the same process with a `send > to` that omits that peer, and an endpoint with `receive { from none; }` |
| `test/plugin/bgp-rs-fastpath-relay-grants.ci` | The route server relays only toward peers that grant it `send [ update ]`: destination E is refused, peer N is receive-only | Restated with a `send > to` on the route server that omits E, and N outside its `receive > from` |
| The ten detector tests: `test/plugin/nexthop-auto-rs-client-unchanged.ci`, `nexthop-auto-ebgp-rewrite.ci`, `bgp-rs-rpki-revalidate-relay.ci`, `bgp-rs-rpki-revalidate-relay-forked.ci`, `bgp-rs-rpki-pending-withdraw-relay.ci`, `bgp-rs-replay-late-destination.ci`, `bgp-rr-rpki-revalidate-relay.ci`, `bgp-rr-rpki-pending-withdraw-relay.ci`, `bgp-rr-replay-late-destination.ci`, `adj-rib-in-filtered-not-replayed.ci` | Each carries `reject=stderr:pattern=send refused`, so a relay sent under the name of a plugin that holds no grant on that peer turns the test red | Each keeps its detector by a `to` on the plugin that must not relay to that peer, or by that plugin's session-type limit; the refusal text changes with the one message shape. `bgp-adj-rib-in` declares `bgp:update`, because it replays today |
| About 40 route-server and reflector `.ci` files with a receive-only source peer in check mode | The source peer receives no relayed UPDATE, because it granted the forwarder no `send [ update ]` | Each is restated with a `to` on the forwarder or keeps its expectation through the forwarder's session-type limit; each is listed before the converter runs |
| `test/exabgp-compat/native/api-multiple-api.conf` | Two plain external scripts with per-neighbor `api { processes }` scope | Each script is an endpoint with `receive > from` and `send > to` (E-1) |
| `test/plugin/exabgp-bridge-shutdown-internal.ci` | The internal runner writes ExaBGP's shutdown line to a text script before the daemon stops | Deleted: `exabgp-bridge-shutdown-external.ci` proves the same of the one remaining runner; the judge confirms it covers the text encoder too, or that test gains a text case |
| `test/reload/exabgp-bridge-run-reload-refused.ci` | A reload changing a bridge script's `run` is refused while the bridge runs | Restated on the script endpoint's `external { run ...; }` line, which `RefuseChangedDeclarations` refuses on reload naming `restart ze` (keeping the external-plugin restart-only owner ruling intact); routed through the judge |
| `test/reload/reload-exabgp-bridge-capability-accepted.ci` | A reload changing the bridge's `route-refresh` is accepted and installed | Follows E-4: the bridge's `route-refresh` leaf is deleted, so the case is deleted with it, or restated on what the translator declares at Stage 3, whose source is the `--route-refresh` option of its `run` line (E-7): a changed `run` line restarts the endpoint |
| `test/parse/range-length-pattern-refusal.ci` | A `length` refusal, sampled on the bridge's `run` leaf | The same refusal on another leaf with a `length` restriction |
| `test/exabgp/*/expected.conf` (six goldens) | The migration output for six ExaBGP configs | Refreshed to the bus form; `process/expected.conf` loses the bridge root for endpoints |
| `internal/plugins/exabgp/bridgeplugin/` (five test files) | The bridge config parser, its reload refusals, its capability reload and the one offline verifier | Deleted with the package (E-1); what they prove of `bridgerun` stays in `bridgerun`'s own tests |
| `internal/plugins/exabgp/bridgerun/respawn_test.go` (3 tests), `bridgerun/owner_test.go` (`TestExitWithdrawsOwnedRoutes`), `internal/exabgp/bridge/bridge_owner_test.go` (4 tests), `TestExitForgetsWatchdogRoutes` in `bridge_watchdog_test.go` | The translator's fork limit (five in a 64-second window), ExaBGP's last-announcer ownership, the withdrawal of an exited script's routes with `restore-configured`, and the watchdog forget before it | Deleted with `respawn.go`, the supervise loop and `OwnerLedger` (E-6). The restart is proved by S42 and the process manager's own tests, the withdrawal by S38 and S42, the watchdog forget by `bgp-watchdog`'s participant-exit test (Q-E11, option (a)), and the compatibility cases `api-on-exit`, `api-on-exit-configured`, `api-on-exit-v4-keep` and `api-no-respawn` keep their expectations from migrated output. The judge confirms nothing they proved is left unproven |
| `internal/component/bgp/reactor/send_permission_test.go` (6 tests), `send_permission_rails_test.go` (6), `forward_rs_permission_test.go` (4), `source_receipt_bound_grant_test.go` | The ten send rails share one per-peer filter, and a source receipt is bound to the per-peer grant | The rails share the compiled filter; each per-peer case is rebuilt with a `to` list |
| `internal/component/bgp/config/config_test.go`, `redistribute_binding_test.go`, `aigp_binding_test.go`, `flowspec_binding_test.go`, `delivery_graph_test.go`; `internal/component/bgp/reactor/delivery_graph_test.go`; `internal/component/plugin/server/delivery_graph_test.go`, `dissemination_test.go`, `dissemination_config_test.go`, `dissemination_validate_test.go`; `internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rfc8212_write_refused_test.go`; `internal/component/bgp/plugins/filter_irr/readmit_test.go` | Each builds or asserts per-peer `send` grants | Rewritten on the compiled filter; each judged for proving no less. A changed RFC-tagged test re-takes its record (`ai/rules/rfc-compliance.md`) |
| The receive census tests (listed under "Receive census") | A program is not fed a peer that does not attach it | Restated with a `receive > from` on the program or a `send > to` on the peer |
| `test/plugin/external-yang-command-line.ci`, `TestFirstPassMergesCommandLinePlugins` | A program named with `--plugin` gives the same outcome as one in the config | Converted only; listed so the conversion is judged as not weakening |
| The AIGP tests that assert a refusal with `aigp` at its default | A narrowed Loc-RIB is refused whenever an iBGP peer exists | The refusal applies only with `aigp` written (S-8); the old expectation is shown wrong against the owner decision |

### Per-peer send census

Taken 2026-11-23 over every tracked config, `.ci`, interop scenario and doc that carries `attach process`. A file is asymmetric when a process holds a `send` list on one peer and a different list, or none, on another.

| Where | Files with a per-peer send asymmetry |
|-------|--------------------------------------|
| `test/plugin/` | 124 |
| `test/interop/scenarios/` | 28 |
| `test/draft/plugin/` | 17 |
| `test/reload/` | 14 |
| `docs/guide/` (`bgp-peering.md`, `configuration.md`, `plugins.md`) | 3 |
| `test/exabgp-compat/native/` (`api-blocklist.conf`, `api-multiple-api.conf`) | 2 |
| `test/draft/reload/` | 2 |
| `test/ui/cli-completion-full-config.ci`, `test/perf/configs/ze.conf`, `docs/config-reference.md` | 1 each |
| Total | 193 |

The 28 interop scenarios: `as-path-mixed-width-relay-frr`, `bgp-addpath-readvertise-collision-frr`, `bgp-aggregator-as4-downgrade-bird`, `bgp-atomic-policy-admission-frr`, `bgp-attr-set-included-as-set-frr-bird`, `bgp-best-route-transit-bird`, `bgp-best-route-transit-frr`, `bgp-flowspec-gobgp`, `bgp-graceful-restart-frr`, `bgp-large-community-no-repeat-gobgp`, `bgp-med-across-as-gobgp`, `bgp-med-ibgp-ordinary-post-selection-removal-gobgp`, `bgp-med-ibgp-post-selection-removal-gobgp`, `bgp-med-remove-configured-gobgp`, `bgp-nexthop-auto-ebgp-frr`, `bgp-nexthop-self-local-auto-frr`, `bgp-paths-limit-best-path-frr`, `bgp-prefix-sid-duplicate-tlv-frr`, `bgp-prefix-sid-malformed-frr`, `bgp-rfc7606-relay-shape-frr`, `bgp-rfc7999-blackhole-readvertise-frr`, `bgp-rfc9774-as-set-frr`, `bgp-role-export-roleless-destination-frr`, `bgp-role-rs-client-learned-not-upward-frr`, `bgp-route-server-replacement-frr`, `bgp-srv6-next-hop-identity-frr`, `bgp-srv6-next-hop-identity-unchanged-frr`, `bgp-vpn-frr`. Each is a scenario whose config the converter changes beyond syntax, so each is named in the interop goal gate.

| Answer | Files | Conversion |
|--------|-------|------------|
| A test of the difference | The two refusal tests, the ten detector tests, `api-multiple-api.conf` and about 40 route-server and reflector tests in check mode | Restated with a list, through the test judge |
| Different lists per peer and no test of the difference | The remaining asymmetric files | Nothing is written: the plugin sends what it declared (M-12) |
| The same list on every peer | Every other file | Converts to nothing, or to one send allow-list where the list is narrower than the declaration |

Per-peer grant read sites, each with its successor:

| Read site | File (symbol) | What it decides today | Per the design |
|-----------|---------------|-----------------------|----------------|
| `filterPermittedPeers` | `internal/component/bgp/reactor/reactor_api.go` (two calls), `reactor_api_forward.go`, `reactor_api_forward_batch.go`, `reactor_api_relay.go`, `reactor_api_selected.go`, `aigp_readvertise.go`, `origin_receipt.go`, `destination_marker.go`, `destination_constraint.go` | Drops each target peer that does not grant the sender the send type | Reads the sender's compiled filter: all peers, or the set its `to` and the peers' `from` leave |
| `bindingsMaySend` | `reactor/origin_runtime.go` (`originWithdrawal`), `reactor/origin_grants.go` (`revokeRemovedOriginGrants`) | Whether an origin withdrawal is allowed on a peer; revocation after a reload removed the grant | Reads the filter; revocation follows a reload that removes the peer from the sender's filter |
| `DeliveryGraph.MaySend` | `internal/component/bgp/server/events.go` (`peerStateUnheldRoles`) | Whether a fed claimant holds the replay role for a peer | Reads the filter |
| `DeliveryGraph.MaySend` | `internal/component/plugin/server/request_cancel.go` (`selectedCancellationScope`), `dispatch_selected_refresh.go` | A `select-routes` or `select-refresh` destination needs the caller's `update` grant; the refusal names the `attach process` line | Reads the filter; the refusal takes the one message shape |
| `DeliveryGraph.MaySend` | `internal/component/plugin/server/dissemination.go`, `dissemination_validate.go` | A peer-output role's grants, judged per peer | Replaced by the declared feature requirement and its one check |
| `requireRIBDelivery` | `internal/component/bgp/config/flowspec_binding.go` | Finds a forwarder that receives `state` and holds `send [ update ]` on the peers | Replaced by the declared feature requirement |
| `ProcessBinding.MayPushRoutes` | `reactor/peer_settings.go`, read by `Peer.initialUpdateReporters` (`reactor/peer_run.go`) | Which processes count as reporters of a session's initial update | Reads the filter: a plugin that may push routes to the peer, is fed its state and signals session-ready. The forwarders' session-type limit keeps that set to the plugins that serve the peer |
| `ProcessBinding.MaySend(update)` | `reactor/peer_bound_info.go` (`boundPeerInfo`), `reactor/peer_forward_facts.go` (`rsRelaySendProcessName`), `reactor/policy_reload.go` (`Peer.policyReplayOwner`) | Each picks the one process that holds `send [ update ]` on the peer | Reads the filter. The pick stays "first in order"; it is single-valued because only one forwarder serves a peer after the session-type limit. Two forwarders an operator deliberately points at one peer both serve it, which M-11 accepts, and doctor reports the peer as served by two forwarders (Q-9, option (a)) |

### Receive census

Taken 2026-11-23 with the per-peer send census, over the same files.

| Finding | Files |
|---------|-------|
| A program attached to fewer than all peers, or with different `receive` lists per peer | 186 |
| of which a program is attached to some peers only | 125 |
| of which the `receive` lists differ per peer | 109 (a file can be in both) |
| A declared external plugin that no peer attaches | 673: the upper bound of the programs that start receiving every peer |

The tests that assert a program did not receive a peer's event go to the test judge:

| Test | What it proves today | What restates it |
|------|----------------------|------------------|
| `test/plugin/attach-process-unattached-is-silent.ci` | An unattached program receives nothing | `receive { from none; }` on its endpoint (S4, Q-1) |
| `test/plugin/rpki-decorator-autoload.ci` | `receive [ update-rpki ]` on `attach process` auto-loads `bgp-rpki-decorator` and `bgp-rpki` | Explicit `bus { endpoint rpki-decorator { internal bgp-rpki-decorator; } }` (Q-11, option (c)); routed through the test judge |
| `test/plugin/attach-process-receive-filter.ci`, `redistribute-explicit-attach-kept.ci`, `bgp-best-route-attach-isolation.ci`, `bgp-best-route-start-destination-send-only.ci` | A program is fed only the events or peers its attachment names | A `receive > allow` and a `receive > from` list on the program (S4, S8) |
| `test/plugin/attach-process-dynamic-group.ci` | A dynamic peer is fed to a program through its group's attachment | A `receive > from` naming the group's path (S22) |
| `test/plugin/attach-process-delivery-graph.ci` | The graph holds one edge per attached peer | `TestDeliveryIndexFromFilters` |
| `test/plugin/attach-process-runtime-subscribe.ci` | `request subscribe` adds a type only inside the peer's grant | Bounded by the registration and the receive filter |
| `test/plugin/adj-rib-in-replay-unowned-peer.ci` | `bgp-adj-rib-in` replays to a peer no forwarder owns | A peer outside the forwarder's session type, or outside its `send > to`: the forwarder's claim is retracted for it and `bgp-adj-rib-in` replays |
| `test/reload/bgp-attach-receive-only-live.ci`, `bgp-attach-dynamic-receive-only-live.ci`, `bgp-attach-grant-change-live.ci`, `bgp-attach-no-route-refresh-restarts.ci`, `bgp-attach-sent-direction-restarts.ci`, `bgp-rpki-attach-live.ci` | A reload that changes a peer's attachment changes delivery live, or restarts the session where it must | A reload that changes a list (S19) |
| `test/interop/scenarios/bgp-attach-receive-only-live-frr` | The same against FRR | A reload that changes a list, rerun against FRR |
| `test/reload/reload-exabgp-bridge-capability-accepted.ci`, `test/plugin/rfc8212-ebgp-no-export-policy.ci` | The bridge attached to some peers only | The script endpoint's `receive > from` (E-1) |

### Fixture command census

Every external test fixture and every in-tree plugin needs a declaration that covers what it sends, or its tests fail closed. Taken 2026-11-23, static:

| Measure | Result |
|---------|--------|
| Fixtures the functional tests start | 827 fixture names, used by 1,093 `.ci` files |
| Where they are registered | 167 source files under `internal/test/fixture/`; 796 names resolved to their source by name, 31 not resolved statically |
| SDK calls in the fixture sources | `UpdateRoute` 51, `DispatchCommand` 48, `RouteMetrics` 7, `RouteInstall` 6, `ReleaseCached` 4, `EmitEvent` 4, `ForwardCached` 3, `RouteRemove` 3, the `State*` calls 5, `RaiseIssue` and `ClearIssue` 3 |
| Literal command prefixes they dispatch | Led by `show bgp` (67), `request shutdown` (22) and `request quiesce` (21) |
| Why the static census is not the per-fixture set | Most fixtures dispatch through about 25 shared wrapper helpers, so a fixture's source does not show which commands it sends |

The complete per-fixture set comes from a runtime capture through the dispatcher's existing plugin accounting (`Username` from `internalPluginIdentity(proc.Name())` and `Sender` from `plugin.ProcessSender(proc.Name())`). A capture over the functional suites records, for each plugin name, every command path and engine operation it used, and the declarations are written from it once.

The brief that ordered this census counted 13 `docs/` and `ai/` files; the grep above finds 14 |

### Interop Tests (Scope: protocol)
No wire format changes. Which plugin sees and sends what does change, so the converted scenarios under `test/interop/` run against their peers and that run is a goal gate. The run names each scenario whose config the converter changed beyond syntax, including the 28 scenarios the per-peer send census lists (each holds a per-peer send asymmetry, so each is restated through the test judge under M-12; all 28 directories exist under `test/interop/scenarios/`, checked 2026-11-23). The route server and reflector session-type limit is proven against FRR by `bgp-route-server-replacement-frr` and one reflector scenario named at implementation.

- Delete `internal/plugins/exabgp/bridgeplugin/` with its YANG module and its five test files (E-1). `bridgerun/` stays: the wrapper runs a Fleet of one script
