## Design presentation

### What changes

Today an operator names a program under `plugin { }` and then, on every BGP peer, writes an `attach process` block that says which events the program is fed and which messages it may send. A program nobody attaches receives nothing and sends nothing, and yet any command a plugin dispatches is authorized without a look at any profile.

After this work everything on the bus is a participant with a name based on its configuration path. A participant says for itself which messages it sends and which it receives, and a compiled-in plugin and a program Ze did not ship say it the same way. With nothing written, that is what runs, on every peer, including a peer added later. The operator overrides it where the participant is configured: `to` replaces where its messages go, `from` limits who may address it, and two allow-lists limit which messages it may send and receive. A third-party program can be limited the same way from the `bus` section. Ze compiles both places into one filter per participant, refuses a disagreement naming both lines, and enforces the filter on events and on every operation a plugin calls, so a plugin can no longer run a command it did not declare. `show ... | implied` shows what was registered beside what was written, including what a written list replaced.

A compiled-in plugin runs in the daemon unless its endpoint says `process separate`, and stays the same plugin either way. `hub` moves under `bus`. An ExaBGP script becomes an ordinary endpoint that runs the wrapper. Every config, test and page in the repository is converted in the same work, and the converter writes no override.

### Before and after

1. **Default wiring: an external collector.**

Before:
```
plugin { external collector { run ./collector.py; encoder json; } }
bgp {
    peer edge1 { attach process collector { receive [ state update-received ]; } }
    peer edge2 { attach process collector { receive [ state update-received ]; } }
}
```
After:
```
bus { endpoint collector { external { run ./collector.py; } } }
bgp { peer edge1 { } peer edge2 { } }
```
The collector receives what it registered from every peer, and a peer added later needs no line.

2. **`send > to`: a lab peer whose routes stay out of the RIB.**

Before: `peer lab { attach process collector { receive [ update-received ]; } }` and no attachment of the RIB on `lab`.
After:
```
bgp { peer lab { bus { send { to [ /bus/endpoint/collector ]; } } } }
```
The list is the whole destination set; `| implied` shows the RIB among the defaults it replaced.

3. **`receive > from`: a collector that hears one group.**

Before: an `attach process collector` block on each peer of the group and on no other peer.
After:
```
bus { endpoint collector { external { run ./collector.py; } receive { from [ /bgp/group/transit ]; } } }
```

4. **Per-message list.**

After only (no equivalent before):
```
bgp { peer lab { bus { send { to [ /bus/endpoint/bgp-rib ]; message bgp:state { to [ /bus/endpoint/collector ]; } } } } }
```

5. **Allow-lists: a program that may only watch state and send nothing.**

Before: `attach process acme { receive [ state ]; }` on every peer, and no `send` list anywhere.
After:
```
bus { endpoint acme { external { run ./acme; } send { allow none; } receive { allow [ bgp:state ]; } } }
```

6. **Two places, one filter.**

After:
```
bgp { route-server { bus { send { to [ /bgp/group/ix ]; } } } }
bus { endpoint bgp-rs { internal bgp-rs; send { to [ /bgp/peer/edge1 ]; } } }
```
is refused at commit, naming both `send > to` lines. Either line alone, or both with the same list, loads.

7. **Commands a plugin may run.**

Before: any plugin, a forked third-party program included, runs `clear bgp peer` with no check.
After: the command is refused unless the program declared `cli:clear bgp peer`, and the operator can cut it further: `send { allow [ "cli:show bgp peer" ]; }`.

8. **Seeing what is implied.**

Before: `show config running` shows only what was written; derived bindings are invisible.
After: `show config running | implied` prints each registered default under a `# implied by:` comment, and beside each written list what it replaced.

9. **Identity of a renamed plugin.**

Before: `plugin { internal my-ddos { use ddos-flowspec; } }` fails to read the plugin's state key, because the grant is keyed on the alias.
After: `bus { endpoint my-ddos { internal ddos-flowspec; process separate; } }` reads it, in the daemon or forked.

10. **Offline validation of an external program's config.**

Before: `ze config validate site.conf` with no daemon answers that the section of the external plugin `acme` is not available.
After: it runs `acme` in query mode and answers whether the config is valid.

11. **ExaBGP (E-1 to E-7 decided; Q-E3, Q-E4, Q-E9 and Q-E11 carry AFK recommendations; Q-E7 awaits measurement on a known host).** A script that exits takes its translator with it, and the endpoint's `external { respawn }` restarts both through Ze's process manager (E-6); `ze exabgp migrate` writes `respawn false` and `external { on-exit keep; }` from the process's `respawn` and `on-exit`.

Before:
```
exabgp { bridge { process p1 { run ./p1.py; encoder json; feed 192.0.2.1 { event [ receive-update ]; } } } }
bgp { peer a { attach process exabgp-bridge { receive [ * ]; send [ * ]; } } }
```
After (using the Q-E4 ExaBGP key table):
```
bus { endpoint p1 { external { run "ze exabgp plugin --encoder json ./p1.py"; } receive { allow [ bgp:update ]; from [ /bgp/peer/a ]; message bgp:update { tag direction [ received ]; } } send { to [ /bgp/peer/a ]; } } }
```

### Alternatives set aside

| Alternative | Why set aside |
|-------------|---------------|
| Flows as named objects in `bus { flow }`, naming receiver and subjects by instance-identifier path (the previous design of this spec) | Superseded by M-1 to M-10: the override sits on the participant, a list replaces rather than narrows, and an address or a prefix of names has no instance-identifier form |
| Allow-lists only, on the participant, with no `send > to` or `receive > from` | Says what may be said or heard, never to whom: "this peer's routes go to the collector only" is not expressible |
| `send > to` and `receive > from` only, with no allow-list | A command's destination is fixed, so a destination list cannot say "these commands only" |
| An add form beside the replacing list | Thomas ruled replacement; an add form and a remove form are a deny list by another name |
| The bus arbitrates exclusive roles (extend `Claims`) | Ruled out by M-11; each forwarding plugin limits itself |
| A second gate before the dispatcher for plugin commands | Two authorizers for one command, and the prefix allowance stays in `authz` |
| Converting each `attach process` block to a `send > to` list | Wrong by M-12: attach adds from silence, `send > to` is exclusive, so a literal conversion would cut the RIB off |
| Three child specs | Thomas ruled one spec; the phases are inside it |

### Documents departed from

1. `docs/architecture/api/architecture.md` ("Owner ruling: implied and shown, or written"): "Where the operator has a choice, which program watches a peer and what it may send, the operator writes it, and silence stays the default." Before: a program nobody attaches receives and sends nothing. After: a participant receives and sends what it registered. Example 1.
2. `docs/architecture/api/architecture.md` ("Owner ruling: endpoints and flows on the bus"): "A BGP group or peer takes part in a flow by listing its name, and the list merges through groups as every list does." Before (as designed there, never built): named flows. After: no flow object; `bgp { }` carries a `bus` block whose lists replace. Examples 2 to 4.
3. The earlier ruling of this spec that `bgp { }` holds no bus wiring ("pure BGP", D-5). Before: nothing of the bus under `bgp { }`. After: a BGP group or peer carries a `bus` block. What still holds: BGP's own YANG module names nothing of the bus, the bus module attaches the grouping, and no engine or config code names a feature. Example 2.
4. The earlier ruling that the send allow-list is per plugin, not per peer (R7, A-Q5). Before: one list on the endpoint. After: one filter per participant, so a peer, a group or a script can carry its own. Example 5.
5. `docs/architecture/api/architecture.md` ("What the Plugin Declares and What the Peer Grants"): "A plugin declares what it CAN handle, in its ready RPC." The declaration moves to the registration and becomes the default.
6. `docs/architecture/api/architecture.md` ("Data Flow: Config to Delivery"): "A relay is judged on the grant of the process that sends it, and on nothing else: no process lends its grant to another." A relay is judged on the sender's compiled filter.
7. `docs/architecture/plugin/plugin-system.md` ("One plugin, two ways to reach the bus"): "With no daemon running, no command starts it, and a config section only its module defines is not available". Example 10. `ai/doors/config-yang.md` and `ai/doors/plugin-infra.md` restate the old rule and are corrected with the page.
8. `docs/architecture/api/process-protocol.md` ("Persistent runtime state"): "Requests use the daemon's configured process name as identity.", ("Owned interface devices and firewall table contribution"): "The owner of every device and table is the authenticated process name, never a name in the request.", and ("Event Delivery"): "the plugin subscribed to the type in that direction, and the peer's `attach process <name>` block grants it." Example 9: compiled-in plugins key state grants, firewall table reservations and interface owned-device grants on their compiled registration identity under any endpoint alias or transport, while foreign external programs hold none of those grants.
9. `docs/architecture/api/architecture.md` ("The Implicit State Grant"): "an `attach process` block for that plugin that lacks `state` gets it, because freeing the member's per-peer state is the plugin's obligation, not the operator's". The implicit `state` grant to a filter owner, which today widens what the operator wrote (`GrantImplicitReceive`). After: the filter owner declares the requirement, and an override that removes it is refused with the completing line; nothing is widened in silence.
10. `docs/architecture/memory/lifetime-contracts.md` ("The four contracts"): "The source lease RPC checks the exact original Peer's explicit receive grant for the authenticated configured process." The lease check reads the compiled filter.
11. `docs/architecture/exabgp-bridge.md` ("ExaBGP Bridge Plugin"): "It has two runners with one behavior: an external SDK-mode process, and an internal in-process runner." With it the sections "The internal runner cannot read the `run` line", "`feed` is the relation ExaBGP models on the neighbor" and "Config root", and, by E-6, "A script that exits is started again, unless `respawn` says otherwise" ("The restart is rate-limited exactly as ExaBGP rate-limits it."): the translator exits with its script and Ze's process manager restarts the endpoint. `docs/exabgp/exabgp-migration.md` ("Key Differences", the process-name paragraph of "Config Migration", "Running ExaBGP Plugins"). `ai/doors/system-plugins.md` ("Boundaries" and the `exabgp { bridge }` row of "Who depends on it from outside"). Before: two runners, the migration writes the bridge root, its `feed` blocks and `attach process exabgp-bridge`, and a process keeps its ExaBGP name. After: one wrapper per script, an endpoint whose `bus` block holds the scope and whose `run` line holds the options, and an endpoint name `ipc.CheckPluginName` accepts. Removed by E-1. Example 11.
12. `ai/doors/system.md`, `ai/doors/plugin-infra.md`, `ai/doors/bgp-engine.md`, `ai/doors/bgp-plugins.md`, `docs/architecture/config/syntax.md` and the pages of the Design documents table marked `departs` for syntax alone (`docs/architecture/core-design.md`, `docs/architecture/firewall/firewall-domain-group.md`, `docs/architecture/fleet-config.md`, `docs/architecture/hub-architecture.md`, `docs/architecture/system-architecture.md`, `docs/guide/ddos-mitigation.md`, `docs/config-reference.md`, `docs/guide/flowspec-protected-router.md`, `docs/architecture/doctor-and-health-checks.md`, `docs/architecture/plugin/rib-storage-design.md`): the `plugin { }`, `attach process` and first-pass root forms they quote, and the configured alias where they name it as the identity.
13. The pages that state the per-peer `attach process` grant as the condition of a delivery or a send: `docs/architecture/api/commands.md` ("Event Subscription Commands"), `docs/architecture/rib-transition.md`, `docs/architecture/bgp/structural-forwarding.md`, `docs/architecture/update-building.md` ("Shared ownership admission"), `docs/architecture/api/update-syntax.md` ("Raw Passthrough Commands") and `docs/architecture/wire/nlri-bgpls.md`. Before: a plugin is fed a peer, or may send to it, only where that peer's `attach process` block grants it. After: it receives and sends what it registered until a `bus` block or an allow-list overrides it (item 1). Each page is updated in the same work.
14. `docs/architecture/plugin/plugin-system.md` ("Registration fields"): Before: the RS fast path checks the source and destination `attach process` blocks, plugins declare `EventTypes` and `SendTypes` lazily, and custom event/send tokens in `attach` lists auto-load their provider (`bgp-rpki-decorator`, `bgp-route-refresh`). After: the fast path reads the compiled bus filter, `EventTypes`/`SendTypes` are replaced by participant `Sends`/`Receives` declarations, and custom event/send token auto-load is deleted (Q-11(c)) so an absent sender leaves a reception dormant and named by `| implied` and `show doctor`.
15. `docs/architecture/api/architecture.md` ("Other command transports"): Before: `"Plugin process name (engine-trusted)"` — every plugin identity carries `aaa.ReservedInternalPrefix` and skips authorization profiles. After: every plugin identity is authorized through an ordinary `authz` profile compiled from its participant filter, using its registration identity on either transport (Example 7).
16. `docs/architecture/api/architecture.md` ("The Two Bindings Ze Derives"): Before: redistribution and selection features derive `ProcessBinding` entries automatically when no explicit attach block covers them. After: derived bindings are deleted; with nothing written the Loc-RIB and orchestrator receive and send by their registrations, and each feature declares a requirement checked against the compiled filters.
17. `docs/architecture/config/transaction-protocol.md` ("Phase 1: Verify"): Before: plugin identity is keyed on the configured alias form (`internal rpki { use bgp-rpki }`), and `CheckReplacePluginCapabilities` refuses any duplicate candidate capability owner before apply. After: identity and transport are separate (`bus { endpoint rpki { internal bgp-rpki; } }`, Example 9), and under E-4 / Q-E9(a) two candidate owners declaring the same capability code with identical payload bytes deduplicate on the wire while differing values toward one peer stay refused before apply.
18. `docs/architecture/api/capability-contract.md` ("Config Validation (✅ DONE)"): Before: `validatePeerProcessCaps` walks `ps.ProcessBindings` and refuses `route-refresh` or `graceful-restart` when no `attach process` block grants `send [ update ]` or `send [ raw ]`. After: `validatePeerProcessCaps` reads the peer's compiled bus filters, which are satisfied by registered defaults when a route-pushing plugin is loaded and refused naming the overriding `bus` line when an override cuts every such sender.
19. `docs/architecture/route-selection.md` ("AIGP session policy"): Before: every iBGP peer activates AIGP's derived RIB bindings and refuses any explicit binding omitting received-UPDATE or state delivery to the RIB even when `aigp` is not written. After: derived bindings are deleted and only explicitly written `aigp` activates the declared requirement; default iBGP AIGP activates no requirement and does not refuse cutting the Loc-RIB while default best-path selection still compares AIGP (S-8, AC-15, S26).
20. `docs/architecture/meta/role.md` ("Learned role lifecycle"): Before: every registered OPEN validator receives a peer's removal independently of filter membership via `addOpenRemovalObservers`. After: peer-removal delivery to OPEN validators is a registered reception governed by the compiled bus filter, shown on `show event delivery` with origin `registered` and excluded if the operator's `receive > allow` omits it (AC-39, S32).
### Decision rows to record

Proposed wording for the `## Design decisions` section of each page, written there once Thomas approves it.

| Page | Decision | It forbids |
|------|----------|------------|
| `docs/architecture/api/architecture.md` | Everything on the bus is a participant named by its configuration path. A participant registers the messages it sends and receives, events and commands alike, and with nothing written that registration is what runs | A default of silence; a second vocabulary for commands; a participant with no path name |
| `docs/architecture/api/architecture.md` | A `send > to` list written on a participant replaces the destinations its messages have by registration; it never adds to them. Present and empty means nobody | An add or remove form; merging a member's list into its group's |
| `docs/architecture/api/architecture.md` | A `receive > from` list limits who may address a participant. Absent means everybody; present and empty means nobody | A default of nobody; a deny form |
| `docs/architecture/api/architecture.md` | A send and a receive allow-list of message names (`send > allow`, `receive > allow`) exist on every participant: absent means everything declared, empty means nothing. The bus enforces them when it receives the message | A deny list; enforcement left to the sender |
| `docs/architecture/api/architecture.md` | What is written on the participant and what is written in the `bus` section compile into one filter per participant. A disagreement between the two places, or between an allow-list and a destination on one participant, is refused at commit naming both lines | Two filters for one participant; a silent precedence between the two places |
| `docs/architecture/api/architecture.md` | The bus does not arbitrate between participants: no exclusive-role rule, no commit refusal and no silent winner. A plugin that must not duplicate another limits itself | A role registry in the bus that picks one plugin |
| `docs/architecture/api/architecture.md` | One display shows every registered default beside what the operator wrote, the defaults a written list replaced, and the line that produced each effective entry | An implied relationship that no display shows |
| `docs/architecture/plugin/plugin-system.md` | With no daemon running, a command that parses a config starts each declared external program in query mode, never its run loop, and validates against its declaration | Refusing a section because no daemon runs; accepting a config that could not be checked |
| `docs/architecture/api/process-protocol.md` | Every engine operation names the message it carries or `none` with a reason, and a plugin identity is authorized by a profile compiled from its filter | An operation with no declaration; authorizing a plugin identity by prefix |
| `docs/architecture/exabgp-bridge.md` | An ExaBGP script is an ordinary bus endpoint that runs the wrapper; the bridge config section and the internal bridge mode do not exist | A second config root or a second runner for ExaBGP scripts |

### Choices for Thomas

Answered by Thomas directly and written into this design: the M, E, V, P and S rows under "Owner decisions governing this spec" (including Q-3 and Q-4 -> M-15, Q-6 and A-19 -> M-18, M-3 refined to exact YANG paths plus `/bgp/ip/<address>`, Q-7 -> M-16, Q-8 -> M-14, Q-E1 -> E-2, Q-E2 -> E-5, Q-E5 -> E-4, Q-E6 -> E-3, Q-E8 and standalone exit -> E-6, and Q-E10 -> E-7).

While Thomas was AFK on 2026-11-24, he directed the main session to think through each remaining question, apply its recommended choice, and record it here for his review before approval. Every row below was checked against the producing code on 2026-11-24 and is written into the design above:

| # | Question | Today (producer) | Applied choice (AFK recommendation for review) | Alternatives set aside |
|---|----------|------------------|------------------------------------------------|------------------------|
| Q-1 | How is "present and empty" written for `send > to`, `receive > from` and the two allow-lists? | `Tree.syncMultiValueToValueLocked` (`config/tree.go:1134-1147`) deletes zero-length leaf-lists; RFC 7950 Section 7.7 has no empty leaf-list | **Option (a): keyword `none`** (`send { to none; }`, `receive { from none; }`, `send { allow none; }`, `receive { allow none; }`; combining `none` with other entries refused at commit). Standard RFC 7950, works identically in brace config, `set`, JSON, YAML and gNMI with no `config.Tree` rewrite, and cannot collide with `/` paths or `:` message names | (b) Presence containers (`send { to { } }`, adds nesting to every non-empty block); (c) `[ ]` empty brackets (non-YANG; requires rewriting `Tree`, serializer, `set` format and gNMI) |
| Q-5 | What makes a peer one the route server serves, and one the reflector serves? | Neither `rs/` nor `rr/` checks `rs-client` or iBGP/eBGP today; reactor `forwardUpdateCore` enforces RFC 4456 between iBGP peers while allowing eBGP sources to feed iBGP RR clients (`bgp-policy-diagnostics-internal.ci`); 101 of 158 `test/plugin/` `.ci` files configuring `bgp-rs` omit `rs-client` | **Option (a):** `bgp-rs` serves `session rs-client` peers (RFC 7947 Section 1, `ze-rs-conf.yang`); `bgp-rr` targets internal (`!isEBGP`) peers (RFC 4456 Section 5-6) while still accepting eBGP sources for eBGP->iBGP distribution. Disjoint target sets (`rs-client` is eBGP, RR targets are iBGP), and cleanly separates `bgp-rs`, `bgp-rr` and `bgp-rib`. The 101 legacy `.ci` files split into true RS tests (gain `rs-client true`) and non-RS eBGP transit tests (move to `bgp-rib`), routed through `ze-test-judge` | (b) `bgp-rs` serves all external peers (would hijack ordinary eBGP transit peers when loaded beside `bgp-rib`); (c) neither limits itself (breaks M-11 with both loaded and nothing written) |
| Q-9 | When an operator deliberately points two forwarders at one peer, `policyReplayOwner` returns the first in order. Acceptable? | `reconcileExportFinalKinds` (`policy_reload.go`) walks the single `bgp-adj-rib-in` table once; wire transforms come from peer settings, not `owner` | **Option (a):** accept first in order and report the peer as served by two forwarders in `show doctor`. Looping claimants would replay the single table twice and would need a new plugin RPC | (b) Each claimant replays its own routes |
| Q-10 | Do an endpoint's own `send`/`receive` fields stay beside `bus { participant /bus/endpoint/<name> }`, and at what rank does `participant /bgp/group/<g>` apply? | No central `participant` block exists | **Options (a) and (i):** both spellings stay for endpoints (judged by refusal 6, identical accepted), keeping `participant <path>` uniform for every participant path (M-15); a `participant` entry naming a group applies at the group's rank so a member's own line replaces it item by item (M-14) and refusal 6 judges same-rank conflicts | (b) Strip `send`/`receive` from `endpoint`; (c) Refuse `/bus/endpoint/` in `participant`; (ii) Apply group `participant` at member rank (would refuse every peer override) |
| Q-11 | What replaces event-token and send-token auto-load (`getUnclaimedEventTypePlugins`, `getUnclaimedSendTypePlugins`), and how do loaders report reasons? | Only 2 plugins set `EventTypes`/`SendTypes`: `bgp-route-refresh` already has `ConfigRoots: ["bgp"]` (making send-token auto-load dead code), and `bgp-rpki-decorator` has only 1 `.ci` test (`rpki-decorator-autoload.ci`) relying on `receive [ update-rpki ]` | **Option (c):** delete both token auto-loaders; an absent sender leaves the reception dormant (M-4), with `\| implied` and `show doctor` naming the plugin and `bus { endpoint ... }` line. Every remaining loader returns its reason as a typed value. `rpki-decorator-autoload.ci` goes through `ze-test-judge` | (a) Auto-load from registered reception; (b) Auto-load from written `allow`/`message` line |
| Q-T1 | Do the 11 owner-registered operations (`RegisterEngineOp`) state a message/reason and join the walk? | `EngineOperation` has no message/exemption field; all 11 ops carry owner-side authorization (`SelectedOwner` + dissemination grant, compile-time firewall/iface grants, or `RegistrationName() == "rib"` + session token) | **Option (a):** yes — add the field to `EngineOperation`, record `none` with each op's verified gate reason on all 11 ops, and walk both registries so no future op ships ungated by omission | (b) Leave `RegisterEngineOp` outside the walk |
| Q-T2 | Which identity do the remaining `proc.Name()` checks move to (firewall tables, iface owned devices, metrics, report-issue, release-cached)? | All five currently pass `proc.Name()` (the operator's alias) | **Producer-verified split:** compile-time `init()` grants (firewall table reservations and interface owned-device grants, plus state keys and doctor snapshots) move to **compiled registration identity** so aliased `internal` endpoints work and foreign `external` endpoints hold none; per-process runtime ledgers (`metrics-publish`, `report-issue`, `release-cached`) stay keyed on **endpoint name (`proc.Name()`)** to preserve per-endpoint quotas and cache-consumer ack matching | Moving all five to registration identity (breaks `release-cached` ack matching and merges quotas); leaving firewall/iface on alias (breaks aliased `internal container`) |
| Q-T3 | Is `batch-validate` judged as the `cli` path it dispatches, or its own message? | Both in-daemon and forked transports call `opBatchValidate` -> `dispatchCommandArgs` with `rpc.BatchValidateCommand` (`request bgp adj-rib-in batch-validate`) | **Option (a):** judged as `cli:request bgp adj-rib-in batch-validate`; remove or gate the legacy test-only `DirectBridge.SetBatchValidate` slot | (b) Separate non-`cli` message |
| Q-T4 | Is `update-route` judged as `bgp:update`, as the `cli` path of its command text, or both? | `opUpdateRoute` calls `Dispatcher.Dispatch("send bgp <peer> ...")` (or `"request peer <peer> borr\|eorr"` for `PeerMarker`), passing through `Store.authorize` | **Option (b): `bgp:update` alone** (and `bgp:refresh` for `PeerMarker: borr\|eorr`), restricting `input.Command` to UPDATE subcommands so it cannot invoke `raw`, and dispatching internally after the bus gate. Judging both would break `send { allow [ bgp:update ]; }` (AC-40/S36) by also demanding `"cli:send bgp"` | (a) Both `bgp:update` and `cli:<path>` |
| Q-T5 | When a `cli` command dispatched via `dispatch-command` also selects peers (e.g. ExaBGP's `send bgp <sel> update text ...`, `request peer <sel> flush`, `clear bgp rib <sel>`), how does `send > to` judge it? | ExaBGP uses `dispatch-command` (verified in `bridgerun/script.go:377-380`), not `update-route`; `Store.authorize` sees `"send bgp <peer> ..."` with the selector at token 3 | **Option (a):** `send > to` judges the peers the command selects (`filterPermittedPeers`), while `send > allow` checks the `cli:` prefix (`"cli:send bgp"`, `"cli:request peer"`, `"cli:clear bgp rib"`), never requiring `/bgp` in `to` | (b) Require `/bgp` in `to` (breaks `send { to [ /bgp/peer/a ]; }`); (c) Rely on Q-T4 alone (does not cover `dispatch-command` callers like ExaBGP) |
| Q-E3 | How does the ExaBGP wrapper learn its scope? | `Translator.Peers` parses IPs via `netip.ParseAddr` (`bridge_command.go:345`); `script.receives` keys on `peer.remote.address`; `servedSelector` currently fails to narrow explicit `*` when `len(t.Peers) > 0` | **Option (a):** the bus hands each participant its compiled filter generically at Stage 2 and at each rebuild, carrying each resolved peer's remote IP beside its path. Also fix `servedSelector` so explicit `neighbor *` / `peer *` narrows to `t.everyPeer()` when `len(t.Peers) > 0`, and consolidate the wrapper's event and command tables into one declaration source in `internal/exabgp/bridge/` | (b) Duplicate scope as wrapper CLI flags (violates single declaration); (c) Address `*` and let bus drop (logs WARN refusal per peer) |
| Q-E4 | Does the bus filter per-neighbor ExaBGP message kinds, or does the wrapper? | Verified full API-key mapping (`neighbor-changes` -> `bgp:state` + `bgp:connected`, `receive-update` -> `bgp:update` tag `direction [ received ]`, etc.) and `bridge.Sessions` dependencies on `context_id`/`as4` and session-down clearing | **Option (a):** the bus filters per message kind using `receive > from`, `receive > allow`, `receive > message <name> > from` and direction tags; stamp `as4`/`context_id` on `bgp:update` events so `overlayASPath` needs no hidden `bgp:negotiated`, and key `bridge.Sessions` by `(peer, connectionID)` so reconnects never reuse a stale `negotiated` block without `bgp:state` | (b) Bus delivers all events of `from` peers and wrapper filters (duplicates filtering outside the bus) |
| Q-E9 | Two endpoints declare different values of one capability toward one real peer: refused, or merged? | `planLocked` and `CheckReplacePluginCapabilities` refuse any second global code; `buildOpenFromSnapshot` does not dedup code 2 or merge code 69; `capability.Negotiate` does last-wins overwrite for code 69 | **Option (a):** identical code + payload bytes deduplicates to one capability on the wire with no conflict; differing values of one capability code toward one real peer are refused at start and at verify naming both endpoints, the capability, the two values and the fix. External endpoint `run`-line changes stay refused on reload per `RefuseChangedDeclarations` (owner ruling) | (b) Silently merge unionable capabilities (violates exact-or-reject and risks ADD-PATH conflicts) |
| Q-E11 | What is the keep option called, and does a watchdog forget step stay before engine withdrawal? | `bgp-watchdog` stores given routes in its own `s.given` map and announces them under `bgp-watchdog`'s connection (`watchdog/given.go`), so `EndOriginOwner` cannot see or withdraw them | **Option (a):** leaf `on-exit` (`withdraw` default \| `keep`) under `external` (`external { on-exit keep; }`), and the watchdog forget step stays via a generic bus participant-exit lifecycle message that `bgp-watchdog` registers to receive and handles with `handleForget(endpointName)` unless `on-exit keep` is set | (b) Drop watchdog forget (breaks ExaBGP `_forget_watchdog_owner` and leaks `s.given` routes) |
| Q-E7 | What do one process, one connection and one event parse per script cost (1, 5 and 20 scripts receiving a full-table feed)? | One `Fleet.Broadcast` reads once per fleet | **Owed measurement on a known host:** cannot report throughput/timing figures from the Linux KVM development VM (`claude`) per `ai/META-DECISIONS.md` ("Test systems and labs"). Must be measured via `./le perf` on Thomas's Mac (or real hardware) before ExaBGP cutover closes; memory allocation figures are verified in Phase 8 | Reporting noisy KVM VM throughput numbers (forbidden by `ai/META-DECISIONS.md`) |
## Implementation Steps

One spec, eight phases. A phase lands as compiling commits; the spec closes when all are in.

1. **Phase: Wiring (MANDATORY FIRST)** -- add the failing functional tests of the Wiring Test table and the failing unit tests `TestBusFilterCompilation`, `TestProcessIdentityByRegistration`, `TestBusCommitRefusals` and `TestEveryEngineOpDeclaresItsMessage`. Record `bus-send-undeclared-refused` red against today's code. Take the runtime fixture capture and the route-server census of A-10, and refresh the ExaBGP bridge section census (taken 2026-11-23).
2. **Phase: Registration** -- add the participant declarations to `registry.Registration`, Stage 1 and the message registry; deliver the catalog at Stage 2; convert every plugin and fixture in the tree; delete `EventTypes`, `SendTypes`, the two lazy registrars, the default event namespace and the three subscription calls.
3. **Phase: Identity** -- split identity from transport; add `process separate`; fix `dispatch_state.go`, `doctor_snapshot.go`, `ddos/flowspec/doctor.go`, `data_rpc.go` and the five sibling sites.
4. **Phase: Schema, loader, first pass and validation** -- delete `container plugin` and `container attach`; add `container bus` and the augmented grouping; regenerate the YANG glue; derive the first-pass roots; the replace-whole marker; the eleven commit refusals; offline validation in query mode; retired hints, listener and graph names, `create bgp peer`.
5. **Phase: Filter, delivery and forwarders** -- compile one filter per participant; build the index from it; delete `ProcessBinding` and its writers; move every per-peer grant read site; limit the route server and the reflector by session type; the declared feature requirement and its check; the AIGP trigger.
6. **Phase: Engine operations and authorization** -- the message field on every operation and the walk; the one check in the shared dispatch path; the profile for the plugin identity; the one refusal shape, counter and doctor code.
7. **Phase: Display** -- the `implied` pipe operation, its text and envelope renderers, the notice, the web rows, the edge origins on `show event delivery` and `ze config graph`.
8. **Phase: ExaBGP, conversion and documentation** -- delete `bridgeplugin/`, its module and the `exabgp` root first; then the wrapper as the only runner and the migration output per "ExaBGP" (with Q-E3, Q-E4, Q-E9 and Q-E11 resolved as recorded above, and Q-E7 measured on a known host), with route ownership per participant (E-2), the translator exiting with its script and the endpoint restarted by the process manager (E-6; `respawn.go`, the supervise loop and `OwnerLedger` deleted first; `bgp-watchdog` forgetting routes via the generic participant-exit lifecycle message), virtual session capabilities (E-4) from the `run` line options the migration writes (E-7) and the per-script API version (E-5); run the converter over the tracked files; restate the judged tests; update the pages, doors and decision rows; run `TestRepositoryConfigsValidate`, the scoped functional suites and the converted interop scenarios.

### Critical Review Checklist
| Check | What to verify for this spec |
|-------|------------------------------|
| Completeness | Every AC-1 to AC-46 has an implementation and a passing test, and every scenario S1 to S44 its named test |
| Filters off the hot path | No path text is parsed, built or compared on a delivery; every list is resolved at commit and at index build |
| No layering | No acceptance of `plugin {`, `attach process` or `exabgp { bridge` remains; no subscription call, `EventTypes`, `SendTypes`, lazy registrar, `ProcessBinding` or prefix allowance for a plugin identity remains |
| Bus isolation | `internal/component/plugin/` and `internal/component/config/` hold no BGP import and no `peer` or `group` word; BGP's YANG names nothing of the bus |
| One API for both kinds | Every rule that differs for an external program names its transport or arrival-order reason on `plugin-system.md` |
| Identity | A foreign program named like a compiled-in plugin holds none of its grants; a forked compiled-in plugin holds all of them |
| One filter | Delivery, the engine-operation check, the authorization profile and the display read the same compiled structure |
| Operator errors and doctor codes | Every refusal is an `opererr` error with its fix on the last line in bus syntax |

### Deliverables Checklist
| Deliverable | Verification method |
|-------------|---------------------|
| No active `plugin {`, `attach process` or `exabgp { bridge` outside `plan/` history and `retired.go` | `git grep -E 'plugin \{|attach process|exabgp \{' test/ docs/ etc/ examples/` |
| All tracked configs validate | `./le test unit pkg path ./internal/component/config/cli/ run TestRepositoryConfigsValidate` |
| Scoped unit and functional suites green | `./le test unit pkg` on changed packages, and `./le test functional parse`, `plugin`, `reload`, `encode`, `exabgp`, `editor`, `managed`, `web` |
| Converted interop scenarios pass | `./le test integration interop` for the scenarios the converter changed |
| YANG glue, tier, grammar, enumeration and doc gates green | `./le yang glue check`, `./le arch tier check`, `./le arch enumeration check`, `./le cli grammar`, `./le plugin declarations check`, `./le doc check all` |

### Security Review Checklist
| Check | What to look for |
|-------|-----------------|
| Identity by name | No site takes a compiled plugin's identity from an endpoint's name or a `run` string; only the `internal` leaf gives it |
| Undeclared message | A message the participant did not declare is refused on every engine operation, with nothing written |
| Prefix allowance | `Store.authorize` allows no `plugin:` identity by prefix; the plugin-less RPC identity's callers are listed |
| Sender | No code path takes the sender from message content |
| Empty lists | A present and empty list refuses everything, and no code path reads it as absent |
| Offline validation runs programs | A program is started in query mode only, and the validate output names each program it asked |
| Operator text off the wire | An external declaration's names are bounded and free of control characters before they reach a message or a registry (`ai/rules/plugins.md`) |

### Failure Routing
| Failure | Route To |
|---------|----------|
| Compilation error | Fix in the phase that introduced it |
| The converter breaks `$PORT` or a `refused=` block | Fix the converter's sentinel handling or triage that test |
| A multi-peer test fails after `attach process` is removed | Decide from the censuses whether it needs a `send > to`, a `receive > from`, an allow-list, or the test judge |
| A compiled-in plugin is refused a command it uses | Its declaration is incomplete: fix the declaration from the runtime capture |
| 3 fix attempts failed | STOP. Report all 3 approaches. Ask the user |

## Design Insights
- Removing `attach > content` also removes peer session bounces that `reflect.DeepEqual` caused over dead leaves.
- The reception a plugin declares already exists, in the ready RPC. Moving it to the registration is what lets an offline reader show it.
- `attach process` already had the right place in the tree and the right inheritance. What was wrong was its default (silence), its direction (it named the receiver from the sender's side only) and its scope (BGP alone).
- The per-peer send grant did two jobs: permission, and choosing which forwarder owns a peer. The filter keeps the first. The second belongs to the forwarders, by session type.
- One compiled filter per participant is what makes the display trustworthy: there is no second computation to differ from what runs.

## Owned journal rows
| Class file | Date | Surface (short) |
|------------|------|-----------------|
| `guard-keyed-on-a-field-its-consumer-ignores.md` | 2026-11-14 | `statestore.PluginKeyReadable`/`Writable`/`PluginList` (`plugin/server/dispatch_state.go`), `PluginDoctorState.Name` (`doctor_snapshot.go`), `ddos/flowspec/doctor.go`, and `data_rpc.go` (`runningOwnerError`) key on configured process alias instead of compiled registry name |
| `escape-hatch-scoped-wider-than-its-justification.md` | 2026-11-23 | `internalPluginIdentity` (`plugin/server/dispatch.go`) stamps `aaa.ReservedInternalPrefix` on every plugin process, a forked program included, and `Store.authorize` (`authz/authz.go`) allows the prefix before any profile lookup; `opRouteInstall` and `opRouteRemove` (`dispatch_route.go`) check no caller permission |

The second row was owned by `spec-bus-3-external-command-gating.md` and moves here with that spec's work. Its Spec cell in `plan/journal/escape-hatch-scoped-wider-than-its-justification.md` names this spec. The unchecked `route-install` and `route-remove` named in the surface are closed by AC-28.

## Key Design Decisions
| Decision | Alternatives Considered | Rationale |
|----------|------------------------|-----------|
| One message model: participants named by path, messages for events and commands, the sender a mandatory tag (M-1, M-2) | Endpoints and flows; a separate vocabulary for commands | Thomas's words; one filter then covers delivery and command gating |
| The override sits on the participant, in one grouping the bus augments (M-5) | A central flow list; a block per component written by hand | Written near the emitter; defined once; BGP's YANG names nothing of the bus |
| `send > to` replaces, `receive > from` limits, both block-level and per message (M-6, M-7, M-8) | Narrowing flows; add and remove forms | Thomas's rulings |
| Allow-lists beside the destination lists, enforced at the bus (M-9) | Destination lists alone | A command's destination is fixed; names need their own list |
| Two places compile to one filter, and a disagreement is refused naming both lines (M-10) | A precedence rule between the places | No hidden winner; the operator fixes the line |
| No arbitration in the bus; forwarders limit themselves by session type (M-11) | Extend `Claims` into exclusive roles | Thomas's ruling; RFC 7947 and RFC 4456 already say whom each serves |
| The converter writes nothing; tests of a difference are restated through the judge (M-12) | One-to-one conversion of attach | Attach adds from silence, `send > to` is exclusive |
| A profile for the plugin identity in the authorizer (M-13) | A second gate; keep the prefix allowance | One authorizer and one matcher; the bypass is deleted where it lives |
| A message field on every engine operation, with a walk test | A hand list of gated operations | A new operation cannot ship ungated by omission |
| Identity and transport are two leaves (S-2) | One predicate; a `run` line that names a compiled plugin | A compiled-in plugin keeps its identity on either transport |
| Bare `\| implied` pipe, a `# implied by:` comment, a JSON and YAML envelope, read-only workbench rows, gNMI written-only | `\| display implied`; `##` marker; implied keys merged into the config | Composes with `\| match`; `##` at column 0 breaks format detection; the written config stays pure and `Get` then `Set` round-trips |
| One declared requirement for features and roles | Plugin-name literals in BGP config code; keep `DisseminationDecl` and add a second declaration | One check and one message shape; config code names no plugin |
| Short slash paths: exact YANG paths for configured nodes plus `/bgp/ip/<address>` for matching any peer by IP (M-3) | RFC 7950 instance-identifier text; `/bgp/peer/<ip>` | Thomas's ruling; exact YANG paths avoid name/IP ambiguity and align with the schema, while `/bgp/ip/<address>` cleanly handles static and dynamic IP lookup |
| One spec, phased | Three child specs | Thomas's ruling |

## Known Limitations
- gNMI `Get` returns the written configuration only.
- The offline view and offline validation refuse a config whose external program cannot be asked for its declaration.
- A plugin that builds a command path from run-time data must declare the prefix it builds under.
- One `message` entry holds one `from` and one tag filter applied together, so the same message with different tag filters depending on the sender is not expressible; left out until a need appears (M-16, M-17).
- FlowSpec authorization refuses a Loc-RIB that runs as a separate process, because its lookups are process-local. That is a transport reason and is stated on `plugin-system.md`.
- The ExaBGP Q-E7 throughput measurement (1, 5 and 20 scripts receiving a full-table feed) awaits a run on Thomas's Mac or real hardware (`ai/META-DECISIONS.md` forbids reporting perf figures from the Linux KVM development VM). Documented differences: `status` reports the wrapper's pid and uptime, `ping` a UUID per wrapper, the shutdown line the wrapper's pid, a script scoped to named neighbors does not reach a peer it creates at run time, as today, `api version` moves its own script only (E-5), a script's exit no longer withdraws a route another script announced (E-2), capabilities are declared per script (E-4), the endpoint is restarted by Ze's process manager within five restarts in 60 seconds and twenty per daemon life, where ExaBGP allows five forks in a 64-second bucket with no lifetime cap (E-6), and a script that moves its own API version at run time no longer changes what its exit does (E-6).

## Implementation Summary

### What Was Implemented
Landed across 19 `Spec-work: explicit-plugin-and-attach-config` commits (`52d4c03aec`..`e8457ed0ce`) plus Commit A closure polish:
- **Phase 1 (`52d4c03aec`, `77b584c1ed`)**: Approved design digest `0551c8168049` recorded in git history; red wiring probes (`bus-send-undeclared-refused` red against the old prefix bypass) and assumption/census audits completed.
- **Phase 2 (`2c7c07e1e6`)**: Unified message registry (`internal/core/events/events.go`), participant declarations (`ParticipantNodes`, `Sends`, `Receives`) on `registry.Registration` and Stage 1 `rpc.DeclareRegistrationInput` (`registry/bus_decl.go`, `stage1_checks.go`), Stage 2 catalog delivery, and deletion of `EventTypes`, `SendTypes`, `ValidSendTypes`, `RegisterSendType`, lazy registrars, default event namespace, and `SetStartupSubscriptions*` across all compiled-in plugins and fixtures.
- **Phase 3 (`9779726169`)**: Split plugin identity from process transport (`PluginConfig.CompiledIdentity()`, `IsCompiled()`, `RunsInDaemon()`, `RunsSeparateProcess()`, `RunsExternalProgram()`, `RunsForked()` in `internal/component/plugin/types.go`), added `process separate` for compiled-in plugins (`internal/component/plugin/process/process.go`), and fixed alias-keying across `dispatch_state.go`, `doctor_snapshot.go`, `ddos/flowspec/doctor.go`, `data_rpc.go`, firewall table reservations, interface owned-device grants, and the five lifecycle helpers (`startup_alias_test.go`).
- **Phase 4 (`34f8c2b490`)**: Replaced `container plugin` and `container attach` with `container bus` (`endpoint`, `participant`, `hub`) and `grouping participant-bus` (`internal/component/plugin/yang/ze-plugin-conf.yang`), synthetic virtual participant augmentation (`ze-bus-participants.yang`), `ze:first-pass` root derivation, `ze:replace-whole` schema-driven group-to-peer inheritance (`internal/component/bgp/config/resolve.go`), all 11 commit refusals (`internal/component/config/bus_validate.go`), offline query-mode validation (`config.PublishDeclaredSchemas` -> `plugin.QueryDeclaration`), retired keyword hints (`retired.go`), `bus-hub` listener/graph naming, and removal of `create bgp peer attach`.
- **Phase 5 (`f6710d6e5f`)**: Compiled one filter per participant (`CompileBus`, `CompiledBus` in `internal/component/plugin/server/bus_filter.go`), rebuilt `DeliveryGraph` from compiled filters with zero-allocation lookups (`TestBusDeliveryAllocatesNothing`, `BenchmarkBusDelivery`), deleted `ProcessBinding` and derived-binding writers, moved all per-peer send-grant read sites, enforced session-type scoping on `bgp-rs` (`session rs-client`, RFC 7947) and `bgp-rr` (internal peers, RFC 4456), implemented declared feature requirements and explicit-only AIGP activation (`aigp_binding_test.go`, `bus_requirement_test.go`), and implemented per-participant route ownership withdrawal on exit with `external { on-exit keep; }` (`origin_runtime.go`, `origin_participant_exit_test.go`).
- **Phase 6 (`f2910a5e23`)**: Added `BusMessage` / `BusExemptReason` to every `engineOps` entry (`dispatch_registry.go`) and all 11 `RegisterEngineOp` entries with an exhaustive walk test (`TestEveryEngineOpDeclaresItsMessage` in `internal/component/plugin/all/engine_ops_walk_test.go`), gated every engine op at shared dispatch (`bus_op_gate.go`), replaced `aaa.ReservedInternalPrefix` allowance in `authz.Store.authorize` (`authz.go:394`) with exact `aaa.ReservedInternalRPCIdentity` + compiled bus `cli:` profile evaluation (`authorizePluginIdentity`, `authz.go:536`), stamped authenticated senders (`ResolveAuthenticatedSender`), and unified the refusal counter and `CodeDoctorPluginRequestRefused` reporting while deleting `ze_bgp_send_refused_total`.
- **Phase 7 (`d5dc519c4e`)**: Registered the `| implied` command-declared pipe operation (`internal/component/config/cli/`), editor/web `ClassifyShowPipes` / `graphicalInspectionWords` / read-only workbench rows, JSON/YAML envelope (`config` + `implied.{endpoints,participants,filters}`) keeping written config pure, `# implied by:` / inline `# implied:` text comments that round-trip cleanly, implied filter-chain member display from the shared seam (`AC-46`, `S43`), offline query-mode `show config source <file> | implied`, and `show event delivery` / `ze config graph` edge origins (`written`, `inherited`, `registered`, `required`, `dormant`).
- **Phase 8 (`b3ed7e62ac`, `e0d8562d69`, `b1eba99cf9`, `7ab06d68db`, `533a0a17bc`, `065e6e2d16`, `955508b41b`, `2d4c16d5fa`, `e09690a57b`, `34813361b3`, `e8457ed0ce`)**: Deleted `internal/plugins/exabgp/bridgeplugin/`, `ze-exabgp-bridge-conf.yang`, `bridgerun/respawn.go`, and `bridge/bridge_owner.go` (`OwnerLedger`); updated `ze exabgp plugin` and `ze exabgp migrate` to emit per-script external endpoints with virtual-session capability flags, per-script API version isolation, process-manager respawn, and watchdog participant-exit cleanup; converted all tracked `.go`, `.ci`, `.et`, `.wb`, `.conf`, 169 interop configs, 45 RFC-tagged `.ci` tests (with fresh host and QEMU guest discrimination proofs and `ze-test-judge-runner` verdicts), promoted all 28+ worked-scenario tests (`S1`–`S44`), and updated all 33 departed architecture docs, guides, 6 subsystem doors, website examples, and all 10 verbatim `## Design decisions` rows + `P-1`.

### Bugs Found/Fixed
- **Alias-keying on state grants, doctor snapshots, and `ze data rm` (owned journal row 2026-11-14)**: Fixed in `9779726169` / `533a0a17bc` by keying state grants, doctor snapshots, firewall table reservations, interface owned-device grants, and the five lifecycle helpers on `proc.RegistrationName()` / `CompiledIdentity()`. Covered by `TestProcessIdentityByRegistration`, `TestAliasedPluginLifecycleHelpers`, and `test/plugin/bus-aliased-forked-state-doctor.ci`.
- **Plugin authorization bypass via `aaa.ReservedInternalPrefix` (owned journal row 2026-11-23)**: Fixed in `f2910a5e23` by restricting `Store.authorize` to exact `aaa.ReservedInternalRPCIdentity`, evaluating plugin identities against compiled `Default: Deny` bus profiles (`authorizePluginIdentity`), and gating all engine operations (`bus_op_gate.go`). Covered by `TestPluginIdentityIsAuthorizedByProfile`, `TestInternalRPCIdentityStaysAllowed`, `TestEveryEngineOpDeclaresItsMessage`, `test/plugin/bus-send-undeclared-refused.ci`, and `test/plugin/bus-send-operation-capability.ci`.
- **Route server / reflector target overlap under default wiring (A-10 / Q-5)**: Fixed in `f6710d6e5f` by restricting `bgp-rs` to `session rs-client` peers and `bgp-rr` to internal targets (`!isEBGP`). Covered by `TestRouteServerServesItsClientSet`, `TestReflectorServesInternalPeers`, and `test/plugin/bus-rs-rr-one-box.ci`.
- **Direct-path peer route withdrawal on participant exit (A-17)**: Fixed in `f6710d6e5f` by extending `EndOriginOwner` to withdraw `originSourceLocalBGP` unless `external { on-exit keep; }` is set and tracking direct-path originated routes per `(peer, prefix, producer)`. Covered by `TestParticipantRoutesWithdrawnWhenItGoes` and `test/plugin/bus-route-ownership.ci`.
- **CLI observer send declaration for RFC 7947 proofs (`34813361b3`)**: Added missing `cli:` observer send declaration uncovered when re-running RFC 7947 discrimination proofs.
- **Closure corpus validation fixes (Commit A)**: Updated `test/plugin/bmp-locrib-external-refuses.ci` expected fix text to `bus { endpoint bmp { internal bgp-bmp; } }`, corrected five `refused=` codes in `bus-{central-participant,dormant-reception,dynamic-peer,tag-filter}.ci`, added `local-address 192.0.2.254;` to `exabgp-s38.conf` in `test/plugin/bus-route-ownership.ci`, and removed the deleted `exabgp-bridge` row and stale source anchor in `docs/DESIGN.md`.

### Documentation Updates
- All 33 distinct `departs` pages in `## Design documents` updated across commits `955508b41b`, `2d4c16d5fa`, `e09690a57b` (verified 0 missing from the `Spec-work` change set).
- All 6 subsystem doors verified current: `ai/doors/{system,plugin-infra,config-yang,bgp-engine,bgp-plugins,system-plugins}.md`.
- All 10 verbatim `## Design decisions` rows + `P-1` verified present in `docs/architecture/{api/architecture.md,api/commands.md,api/process-protocol.md,exabgp-bridge.md,plugin/plugin-system.md}`.
- Removed deleted `exabgp-bridge` row and anchor from `docs/DESIGN.md` (0 stale bus anchors out of 6,089 code paths).
- Operator news fragment added at `website/changes/fragments/explicit-plugin-and-attach-config.md`.
- `./le doc check all`: listed in `Owed commands` for main-thread execution on Commit A.

### Deviations from Plan
- **A-5 (unified bidirectional message names)**: Unified `bgp:update` and `bgp:refresh` as single bidirectional messages in namespace `bgp` carrying closed tag `direction [ received | sent ]` under M-16/M-17/Q-E4 rather than separate event/send names.
- **A-6 (dynamic participant augmentation)**: Implemented via deterministic synthetic virtual YANG module `ze-bus-participants.yang` emitted immediately before `l.Resolve()` in `config/yang`.
- **A-7 (empty lists)**: Resolved by Q-1 option (a): explicit `none` keyword (`to none`, `from none`, `allow none`) in place of empty brackets `[ ]`, preserving RFC 7950 compliance and `config.Tree` / `set` / gNMI round-trip.
- **A-13 (feature requirement conditions)**: Retained `DisseminationDecl` (adapted to bus message names) for transit roles and generic filter-chain owner check for `state`, plus a small typed bus-requirement condition enum (top-level root predicate for redistribute, family SAFI for FlowSpec, explicit peer path for AIGP) instead of forcing non-peer predicates into `FilterInsertion`.
- **Host-gated verification runs**: Live Docker interop execution of the 169 converted scenarios and the Q-E7 host throughput measurement (`./le perf` with 1/5/20 ExaBGP scripts) cannot run or be reported from the Linux KVM development VM (`ai/META-DECISIONS.md`) and are tracked as owed Mac/CI runs.

|------|---------------|----------------------|----------------|--------|
| assumption | A-4 assumed no repo test depended on per-peer send asymmetry; A-7 assumed `[ ]` empty leaf-lists round-tripped; A-13 assumed `FilterInsertion` conditions covered top-level redistribute/FlowSpec; A-17 assumed `endOwner` already withdrew local BGP origins | Per-peer send census found 193 asymmetric files (triaged under M-12/test-judge); `Tree.syncMultiValueToValueLocked` drops empty leaf-lists (resolved by Q-1 `none`); redistribute/FlowSpec needed a typed condition enum; `endOwner` parked `originSourceLocalBGP` and direct peers had no per-producer table | Phase 1 census & producer reads before coding | Fixed in Phases 4, 5, and 8; recorded in Assumptions table and Deviations |
| approach | Five worked-scenario `.ci` tests (`bus-{central-participant,dormant-reception,dynamic-peer,tag-filter}.ci`) used `refused=config-parse` instead of the exact corpus refusal category, `bus-route-ownership.ci` omitted `local-address` in `exabgp-s38.conf`, `bmp-locrib-external-refuses.ci` kept old `plugin { internal bmp ... }` fix text, and `docs/DESIGN.md` retained the deleted `exabgp-bridge` row | `TestRepositoryConfigsValidate` and doc-anchor checks surfaced all 7 items before closure | Main-thread deliverables gate (`TestRepositoryConfigsValidate`, doc anchor check) | Fixed in Commit A and verified green on `TestRepositoryConfigsValidate` and `bus-route-ownership` |

## Core Insight
Separating **compiled registration identity** from **endpoint alias and process transport**, and compiling participant registrations plus operator overrides into **one filter structure (`CompiledBus`)** shared by delivery, engine-operation gating, `authz` command profiles, and `| implied`, eliminates three entire classes of silent drift (alias-keying defects, prefix authorization bypasses, and hidden derived bindings) at once.
